# In pursuance of subSection (5B) of Section 18 of the Chartered Accountants Act 1949 a copy of the audited accounts and the Report of Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) for the year ended 31st March 2025 is hereby published for general information

NOTIFICATION New Delhi, the 31st July, 2025 No.1-CA(5)/76/2025.—In pursuance of sub-Section (5B) of Section 18 of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, a copy of the audited accounts and the Report of Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India

(ICAI) for the year ended 31st March 2025 is hereby published for general information. 76th Annual Report The year 2024–25 will be remembered as a landmark period, one defined by bold ideas, transformative actions and commitment to shaping the future of our profession. As ICAI proudly celebrates 75 years of excellence, we stand at a pivotal moment, looking ahead to 'India@100' with renewed purpose and passion. At the heart of ICAI's initiatives lied the core of Technology, Research, Union Development, Sustainability, and Transparency, setting the profession on a course toward that will shape the next 25 years of the profession. Over the years ICAI has evolved from its role as regulator to emerge as the largest accounting body globally. Today, the accounting profession stands at the crossroads of tradition and transformation, ICAI carries forward its role not just as a regulator, but as a catalyst championing change for value creation. With a renewed sense of purpose, we are not just to modernize practice, but to reimagine the future of finance itself.

As an institution, ICAI is building more than professionals, cultivating problem-solvers, sustainability champions, and digital thinkers who are ready to lead in a world that demands agility and accountability. The Institute is strengthening governance, evolving accounting frameworks, and actively enabling the transition to a green economy, all with a clear conviction - to shape a profession that leads with integrity, creates real value for society, and drives the nation forward with purpose and pride. With India now standing tall as the world's fourth-largest economy and steadily progressing toward the ambitious goal of becoming a $30 trillion powerhouse, the nation is redefining global leadership. In this transformative era, ICAI remains deeply committed to being a trusted partner in nation building, firmly rooted in its role as a guardian of public interest and a catalyst for sustainable progress.

Renowned globally for its ethics, technical excellence, and rigorous education, ICAI continues to shape professionals who are not only financially adept but also socially responsible. Chartered Accountants have long been vital contributors to India's economic story as advisors, enablers, and changemakers across industries and communities. As look ahead, ICAI stands ready to lead with integrity, foster innovation, and nurture talent that can thrive in a dynamic, technology-driven world. With innovative ideas and commitment to excellence, the Institute is shaping the narrative of India's global rise. Over the past 75 years, ICAI has not only upheld the trust of the nation but also helped shape its financial narrative by building a legacy of credibility, excellence, and national service. As India emerges as the fourth-largest economy in the world and sets its sights on becoming a $30 trillion economic force, the role of the accounting profession is more crucial than ever.

Today, the Institute stands at a powerful intersection of legacy and change. With a deep commitment to ethical leadership and world-class technical education, ICAI continues to equip Chartered Accountants with the capabilities needed for a fast-evolving global economy one shaped by technology, sustainability, and shifting governance paradigms. From ensuring transparency in financial systems to supporting businesses in navigating complexity, Chartered Accountants have been at the core of supporting India's growth journey. With the rise of new economic models, digital ecosystems, and green finance reshaping the future, ICAI is ready to lead the way in national development and nurture professionals who will play a vital role in shaping India's future economy.

It is with great pride and purpose the Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) presents the 76th Annual Report for the year ended 31st March 2025.

From its inception on 1st July 1949, ICAI has evolved into a global force now representing over 4.5 lakh members and 10 lakh students, with a presence in 85 international cities across 47 countries. This remarkable journey of ours in service to the nation has been powered by the enduring values of trust, accountability, and professional. The report offers a detailed account of the Council's key initiatives and committee-led activities undertaken during the financial year 2024–2025, along with the Institute's audited financial performance for the year. Reflecting on the milestones of the past year, the Council extends heartfelt appreciation to the members and students for their commitment to ethics, independence, integrity, and excellence, as the profession has continued to grow in relevance and impact on the global stage.

This Annual Report highlights the important progress which have been made and outlines the priorities that will guide ICAI's path forward focusing on innovation, greater impact, and strong leadership in a changing world: 75 Years of Trust- Celebrating the Excellence, Independence and Integrity of the CA Profession ICAI celebrated 1st July 2024 as 75 years of trust, honouring Chartered Accountants hard work and contributions to national development. The event was marked with great enthusiasm and splendor, showcasing the achievements of Chartered Accountants and future plans in the Kartavya Kaal to realize the vision of Viksit Bharat. The grand event, held in the largest hall of Bharat Mandapam, was attended by numerous dignitaries and all CAs. Among them were Minister of Road Transport and Highways of India; Attorney General of India; Secretary of the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology; Secretary of the Ministry of Corporate Affairs, President of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of Bangladesh; Confederation of Asian and Pacific Accountants (CAPA) President; Shri Heshana Kuruppu President of South Asian Federation of Accountants (SAFA).

ICAI unites for a nationwide 'CA Run for Viksit Bharat' The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), the world's largest accountancy body, commemorated its 75 years of service to the nation with the grand 'CA Run for Viksit Bharat' on 30th June, 2024. The event underscored ICAI's commitment to Prime Minister Narendra Modi's vision of transforming India into a developed nation by

2047. More than 10000 Participants participated, including members and students from across the globe, began the run at 6:00 AM from 5 Regional Councils, 176 Branches, and various Chapters. In New Delhi, the run commenced from ICAI Headquarters and concluded at Major Dhyan Chand National Stadium. The event was graced by the presence of Shri Harsh Malhotra, Hon'ble Minister of State of Corporate Affairs, who praised ICAI's contributions to the nation's financial standards. ICAI leadership emphasized the profession's role in economic reforms and good governance and highlighted the collective dedication of the CA fraternity towards a prosperous India.

World Forum of Accountants -2025 The World Forum of Accountants (WOFA) 2025, a prestigious global convergence of accounting professionals and thought leaders, was organized by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) and inaugurated by Hon'ble Defence Minister of India. Held from January 31 to February 2, 2025, at Yashobhoomi, New Delhi, the event brought together over 6,500 delegates from 46 countries — marking one of the largest and most significant gatherings of the global accounting fraternity. Centered on the theme "Accountability Meets Innovation (AI): For a Sustainable Planet," WOFA 2025 served as a dynamic platform for exchanging insights on emerging global trends, sustainable practices, and the transformative role of technology in the accounting profession. More than just a conference, WOFA became a moment of pride and powerful assertion of India's growing stature in the global financial ecosystem. With this landmark event, ICAI showcased India's capabilities as a rising global hub for accounting and auditing services. The Forum reflected India's vision of becoming a knowledge leader in assurance, transparency, and financial stewardship,aligned with the broader national aspiration of positioning India as a trusted voice and destination in the global economy.

Training of Indian Corporate Law Services (ICLS) Officials Being a premier educational body the ICAI continuously endeavours to support the capacity and competency development of government officials for building their understanding on various emerging aspects of financial reporting and laws as well as the evolving best practices. In this direction, the institute has conducted training for ICLS officials 2022 batch at ICAI Head Quarters, New Delhi on 20th April, 2024. With these initiatives the institute aspires to strengthen the process of developing effective and efficient policy for better governance.

ICAI adopts Tech for enhancing Financial Literacy – Finfluencer Meet Surveys reveal that only 27 per cent of India's population is financially literate. Additionally, only 16.7 per cent of Indian students have a basic understanding of finance and money management. As the world's largest accounting body, ICAI as its social responsibility is educating and empowering the citizens of the country and create a sustainable financial ecosystem for the nation to emerge as a self-reliant nation. Taking forward the Government of India's mission of 'VitiyaSaksharta' (Financial Literacy) to the next level and promoting understanding of tax laws, accounting, and diverse facets of the financial system. The institute has organized a Finfluencers meet in New Delhi on 5th April, 2024 wherein 41 prominent social media influencers came forward to collaborate and on educating masses on financial knowledge.

Academia Meet with Universities & Institutions With a vision to make India Vishwaguru, the ICAI organised the Academia Meet with Universities and Institutions held on May 17, 2024, in New Delhi. The event served as a vital platform for meaningful dialogue between ICAI and academia, bringing together scholars, researchers, and senior faculty from over 35 leading universities and institutions across the country. The discussions focused on fostering a collaborative ecosystem for academic research, aligning curriculum with evolving industry needs, and exploring joint initiatives to promote innovation, ethics, and excellence in commerce and accountancy education. This engagement reflects ICAI's firm commitment to strengthening academic partnerships, encouraging applied research, and shaping future-ready professionals through an enriched and forward-looking education framework.

Resolve 2024- 2nd International Convention on Insolvency & Valuation The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) organized the 2nd edition of RESOLVE-2024, the International Convention on Insolvency Resolution and Valuation at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, India in collaboration with the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India, the Indian Institute of Insolvency Professionals of ICAI and the Registered Valuers Organisation of ICAI. The theme of the conference "Enabling Resolution Maximizing Value" aimed to foster the adoption of global best practices in the industry. The two-day event hosted over 40 eminent speakers across 14 technical sessions covering the most relevant and contemporary topics in the space of insolvency resolution. The event brought together over 500 insolvency and valuation professionals, making it the largest insolvency convention in India.4th Global Education Summit in Commerce & Accountancy.

Global Education Summit in Commerce and Accountancy (GESCA) 2024 The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) organised the 4th Global Education Summit in Commerce and Accountancy (GESCA) 2024 at New Delhi, on 5th & 6th July, 2024. The two-day summit was inaugurated by Hon'ble Minister of State for Corporate Affairs and Road Transport and Highways, along with the then President of ICAI. Also present were distinguish Guests of Honor, Vice Chairman of AICTE and Vice Chancellor of NIEPA.

The summit brought together Vice Chancellors, Chairmen, Directors, Principals, Deans, HoDs, and Professors from renowned Indian universities and colleges specializing in Commerce and Accountancy. The focus was on sharing insights to enhance the employability of Commerce graduates through upskilling initiatives. The GESCA 2024 had participation from 36 Vice-Chancellors of various universities, along with representatives from 115 top universities and 30 autonomous colleges across the country.

ICAI AI Innovation Summit 2024 The Institute hosted AI Innovation Summit – AIS 2024 in Hyderabad on July 16th and 17th, 2024. The summit highlighted ICAI's commitment to promote forward-thinking strategies and leverage technology for innovation in accounting, auditing, taxation, and governance. AIS 2024 aimed to be a catalyst for shaping the future of accounting and financial sectors, contributing significantly to the growth of the Indian economy. The two-day flagship event featured panel discussions and thought-provoking sessions by global AI experts, tech industry leaders, policymakers, and academicians. These discussions delved into crucial aspects of exploring the transformative potential of artificial intelligence (AI) in the fields of accounting, audit, tax, and governance. During the summit, important initiatives' ICAI CA GPT' for students, Certificate Course on AI for Members and Digital Publication of 100 AI Tools were launched.

Local Governance Synergy Conclave 'Sashakt Bharat: An Initiative to Strengthen Local-Self Government Accounting' With aim to foster collaboration and innovation in the realm of local governance the Institute organized Local Governance Synergy Conclave themed "Sashakt Bharat: An Initiative to Strengthen Local-Self Government Accounting" on 1st to 2nd October, 2024 in association with O/o C&AG. With over 350 officials from local bodies across India participating, the conclave aspired to bolster transparency and efficiency in local self-governance, aligning it with the vision of a Viksit Bharat. The event was graced by the then Hon'ble C&AG as Chief Guest.

Global Ethics Day ICAI marked 'Global Ethics Day' with a solemn and forward-looking celebration in New Delhi on October 16,

2024. The occasion featured insightful technical sessions and panel discussions centered on the evolving dimensions of ethical conduct in the accounting profession. The event served as a powerful reminder of the Chartered Accountancy profession's deep-rooted commitment to integrity, independence, and public trust. In an era defined by transparency and accountability, ICAI continues to lead by example by championing ethics not just as a principle, but as a foundation for resilient institutions, responsible governance, and a credible financial ecosystem.

ITEC Training Program for Foreign Officials The ICAI has been actively involved in the development of accounting profession globally and keeps on collaborating with government and institutions to support capacity development program for other countries. The ICAI organised its 1st ITEC program under the auspices Ministry of External Affairs with 17 participants from 11 countries at ICAI Centre of Excellence, Hyderabad on November, 18 2024. The then President, ICAI with OSD, Ministry of External Affairs, the then Central Council Member & other dignitaries graced the event.

Interactive Meet with Trade Officers The ICAI keeps on engaging with the global stakeholders to understand the evolving global paradigm and promote India as Global Finance & Accounting Hub. In this context an Interactive Meet with Trade Officers of Foreign Embassies was held which was graced by Guest of Honour Shri Mahaveer Singhvi, Jt. Secretary, Ministry of External Affairs, the then President-ICAI, the then Central Council Member and Secretary, ICAI on 8th November, 2024 in New Delhi. 26 Trade Commissioners/Officers from 23 different embassies attended the event.

ICAI enters into MoU with CBSE The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) through its Committee on Career Counselling signed a MoU with the Department of Skill Education, Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) on 21st November 2024, at Bhubaneswar, Odisha, to promote commerce-based skill courses for students across the country. The partnership aims to foster cooperation between ICAI and CBSE to advance commerce-oriented skills, specifically in the BFSI sector. The prime focus will be on enhancing the skills and employability of students through specialized courses designed to align with industry requirements.

Supporting Government Accounting Reforms - Strengthening Financial Management in Indian Railways The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), through its Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF), has been at the forefront of driving financial reforms in Indian Railways, significantly enhancing its financial management framework. Since 2014, ICAI ARF has served as the technical partner in Indian Railways' ambitious Accounting Reforms initiative, marking a major shift towards transparency, accountability, and efficiency. As a part of this collaboration, ICAI ARF has successfully developed and submitted accrual-based financial statements for Indian Railways for six consecutive years (2017–18 to 2022–23). This transition from cash-based to accrualbased accounting has enabled Indian Railways to gain deeper insights into asset and liability management, better evaluate performance, and improve financial decision-making.

ICAI – Moving Towards Sustainable Planet The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) is leading efforts towards a sustainable future by introducing initiatives like SSAE 3000 and SAE 3410 for sustainability and greenhouse gas assurance, and the Sustainability Reporting Maturity Model. ICAI promotes accountability through its Sustainability Reporting Awards and the Institute of Social Auditors of India, which issues thematic Social Audit Standards. The Green Mahotsav campaign pledges to plant 100,000 trees, symbolizing environmental commitment. This initiative also resonated with national leadership's campaign 'Ek Ped Ma Ke Naam', which encourages every citizen to plant a tree in the name of their mother. ICAI also developed a Carbon Emission Calculator covering Scope 1, 2, and 3 emissions to help individuals and organizations measure and reduce their carbon footprint. Additionally, ICAI supports the LiFE (Lifestyle for Environment) initiative, encouraging mindful consumption and responsible living among its vast member and student network. Through these efforts, ICAI aims to inspire sustainable practices and lead impactful environmental change.

ICAI MSME & Startup Yatra in Asia and India Book of Records The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) is dedicated to empowering MSMEs, women, and youth through initiatives like Startup Sphere, Startup Manthan and MSME and Startup Yatra, fostering entrepreneurship and job creation. Recognized in the Asia and India Book of Records for awareness drives in over 100 cities within 100 days, ICAI promotes a self-reliant economy through key events such as the MSME & Startup Conclave on 26th & 27th June, 2025 and the nationwide MSME Mahotsav on 27th June, 2025. The conclave gathered over 3,000 stakeholders to support innovation and capacity building, while the Mahotsav offered free Udyam registration, consultations, and grievance redressal for MSMEs. Additionally, ICAI signed an MoU with the National Small Industries Corporation (NSIC) on June 27, 2025, to enhance MSME capacity building, entrepreneurship development, and integrated support services including marketing, technology, finance, and global outreach. These initiatives highlight ICAI's pivotal role in advancing India's MSME and startup ecosystem.

ICAI Reforms Exam Structure - More Chances, More Success To align with global best practices and offer students more opportunities, the 26th Council of ICAI has decided to conduct the CA Final exam three times a year instead of twice. Last year, ICAI made a similar change for the Intermediate and Foundation exams, and now all three levels will have equal exam attempts in January, May, and September. Additionally, the Post Qualification Course in Information Systems Audit will also shift from biannual to thrice-yearly assessments in February, June, and October. These changes aim to enhance flexibility, accessibility, and professional growth for students and members. These historic decisions will ensure that both students and professionals have abundant opportunities to succeed and excel in their careers.

ICAI Sets New Benchmarks in Skill Enrichment and Entrepreneurship Development ICAI has been recognized by the India Book of Records and Asia Book of Records for two major achievements i.e conducting 97 entrepreneurship skill programs in a single day and 79 skill enrichment programs within 15 days.

These milestones highlight ICAI's strong commitment to fostering entrepreneurship, continuous learning, and building a future-ready workforce in the accounting and finance sectors.

Integrating Technology: Transforming the Profession In 2024–25, ICAI launched the Committee on Artificial Intelligence to drive AI adoption in education, operations, and member services. Key initiatives included the AICA Certificate Course (150+ batches, 5,000+ members), 19 CA GPT modules benefiting 1.2 lakh users, and specialized GPTs for regulatory tasks. ICAI also advanced digital transformation through e-Office, centralized document management, real-time dashboards, and the 'ICAI Diaspora' mobile app for overseas members. Collaborating with MeitY on DigiLocker, ICAI continues to modernize and lead the profession in the digital age. It has introduced the AI-powered 'ICAI AI Agent' digital workspace, with plans to extend it to members and students. Additionally, ICAI developed the world's first Forensic Accounting and Investigating Standards to enhance digital financial reporting and is working towards their global adoption, positioning itself at the forefront of AI integration in accounting.

Enhancing Expertise, Elevating the Profession ICAI reinforced its commitment to audit quality and ethics by launching the Audit Quality Maturity Model (AQMM v2.0) and hosting the first-ever Global Ethics Day and Audit Day. The UDIN portal surpassed 8 crore generated UDINs with over 1.5 lakh members registered, while the Peer Review Board issued 2,971 certificates in 2024-25. Local bodies received awards for best financial statements, promoting better reporting. To prepare members for future challenges, ICAI introduced various certificate courses in fund management, capital market compliance, skill enrichment, mediation, and an online German language course. Collaborations with institutions like ISB brought leadership masterclasses, and new programs focused on family businesses, professional skill development, and youth entrepreneurship were launched.

Inauguration of ICAI 3rd Centre of Excellence, Kolkata On February 7, 2025, ICAI inaugurated its 3rd Centre of Excellence in Kolkata, following Hyderabad and Jaipur.

This state-of-the-art center aims to enhance professional excellence through advanced learning, research, and skill development, reinforcing ICAI's commitment to empowering finance professionals and contributing to nationbuilding.

Partnering for National Progress • Representation on the Consultative Group on Tax Policy This year, ICAI exemplified this spirit by actively collaborating with the government to align its initiatives with the vision of Viksit Bharat @2047 and contribute meaningfully to national priorities. Representation on the Consultative Group on Tax Policy at NITI Aayog exemplifies Institute's commitment to refining tax policies, simplifying processes, and improving compliance. To address the growing demand for improved financial management in local governments, ICAI signed an MoU with the Comptroller and Auditor General's office launching certificate courses for accountants in Panchayats and Municipal Bodies in which around 4000 youths have registered. • Establishing India as Global Finance and Accounting Hub To position India as a global hub for Finance and Accounting, the ICAI-led IFSCA Expert Committee has recommended establishing GIFT IFSC as a center for Bookkeeping, Accounting, Taxation, and Financial Crime Compliance (BATF) services. ICAI supports this vision by contributing to the Standing Committee on professional services development at GIFT IFSC and promoting global outreach through its international chapters. Additionally, ICAI formed a dedicated group to promote India as an Accounting GCC destination and launched a Nationwide GCC Summit Series across major cities in collaboration with key government and industry stakeholders to build a skilled, GCC-ready workforce. • Supporting for Regional Development in J&K and the North-East ICAI is actively supporting the Government of India's vision for balanced regional development, especially in Jammu & Kashmir, Ladakh, and the North-Eastern states. It participated in the IICA North-East Conclave 2025 in Shillong to highlight regional entrepreneurial potential. ICAI promotes knowledge-sharing and partnerships aligned with local aspirations. Additionally, it has extended a 75% fee concession for CA courses (Foundation, Intermediate, Final) for students from these regions until March 31, 2028, reflecting its commitment to accessibility, inclusion, and socio-economic empowerment. • Building a Financially Empowered India: Vision for Inclusive Growth ICAI is spearheading financial literacy efforts to support India's Viksit Bharat vision, emphasizing inclusive and sustainable growth. Through initiatives like Vittiya Gyan – ICAI Ka Abhiyaan and Vittiya Gyan Mela 2024 across 63 branches, the institute promotes budgeting, investment, and tax awareness at the grassroots. Special programs for Self-Help Groups in Meghalaya and tribal communities further extended outreach. ICAI has conducted over 8,837 Investor Awareness Programs, benefiting 4.43 lakh people—44% of whom are women, youth, and armed forces. It also launched e-learning for government officials via iGOT Karmayogi. With only 27% of Indians financially literate, ICAI aims to empower citizens to move from saving to smart investing, fostering a self-reliant and financially informed nation. • Advancing Governance Through Institutional Capacity Building The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) plays a key role in enhancing governance through capacity building and institutional support. Working with Central and State Governments and public institutions, ICAI has conducted training on modern accounting and GST across various states, often in local languages to ensure inclusivity. Through strategic partnerships and ongoing technical support, ICAI actively contributes to public financial reforms and administrative efficiency, reinforcing its position as a trusted partner in national development. • Empowering Bharat: The Role of Impact Finance and the Rural Economy The soul of India lies in its villages, and building a Viksit Bharat requires uplifting rural communities. Impact finance is a vital tool to turn rural aspirations into sustainable progress. The ICAI's initiative to form dedicated groups for promoting impact finance and the rural economy is a significant step. By guiding rural enterprises, empowering self-help groups, and sharing financial expertise, professionals are driving real change at the grassroots level. • Initiatives for Future Moving towards future ICAI is setting up the ICAI International ADR Centre, following the advice of the Ministry of Law & Justice, this will provide new avenues for professional development. Additionally, ICAI assisted the MCA in streamlining the PM Internship Scheme (PMIS) Implementation announced in the 202425 Budget and further proposed to MCA to have CA firms under PMIS, for empower of youth. ICAI is supporting the Parliamentary Committee in evaluating the economic impact of the "One Nation, One Election" proposal, focusing on cost savings, governance efficiency, and fiscal discipline.

14.

Appreciation Composition of the Council Audited Annual Accounts

THE COUNCIL The Twenty-Sixth Council was constituted on 12th February 2025. It comprises of 32 elected members and 8 members nominated by the Central Government. Composition of the 26th Council is disclosed separately. 11 Council meetings were held during duing financial year 2024-25.

1.

COMMITTEES OF THE COUNCIL The Council in terms of Section 17 of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 constitutes every year on 12th February; various Standing and Non-Standing Committees/Boards and Groups to regulate and develop the profession of Chartered Accountancy. During the financial year ended 31st March 2025, 396 meetings of the said Standing and Non-Standing Committees / Boards and Groups were held.

2.

AUDITORS M/s Arun K Agarwal & Associates & M/s S.K. Mittal & Co were the joint auditors of the Institute for the financial year 2024-25 appointed from the panel maintained by Comptroller and Auditor General of India. The Council wishes to place on records its appreciation of the services rendered by them.

3.

STANDING COMMITTEES The Council of ICAI has the following Standing Committees:

4.1 Executive Committee

4.2 Finance Committee

4.3 Examination Committtee

3.1 Executive Committee The functions of this Committee include matters relating to articled and audit assistants and enrolment, removal, restoration of members from the Register, cancellation of certificate of practice, permission to engage in any other business or occupation other than profession of accountancy. Executive Committee is also the custodian of the property, assets and funds of the Institute beside maintenance of the Institute's offices. The Executive Committee met 6 times during the year.

3.2 Finance Committee This Finance Committee controls, implements and supervises the activities related with and incidental, inter alia, to maintenance of true and correct accounts, formulation of annual budget, investment of the funds, and disbursements from the funds for expenditure – both Revenue and Capital Nature. The Finance Committee met 4 times during the year.

3.3 Examination Committee In line with the vision of Hon'ble Prime Minister, Shri Narendra Modi to make India US$ 5 Trillion Economy by 2030 and realizing the need of increasing requirement of Chartered Accountants to support the growing demand of Indian economy, the Examination Committee of the ICAI decided to hold CA Examinations four times in a calendar year i.e. in the month of January, May, September and November at more than 500 centers across the country and 8 overseas centres in one session from 2 pm to 5 pm (IST).

(I) CONDUCT OF FOUNDATION, INTERMEDIATE, FINAL AND PQC EXAMINATION The Chartered Accountants Examinations for students (Foundation, Intermediate and Final) as well as for Members under Post Qualification Course [International Taxation Assessment Test (INTT-AT) and Insurance and Risk Management Technical Examination (IRM)] were conducted smoothly all over the country and abroad with social distancing norms in the year 2024 -2025 as per below mentioned schedule: - No of students admitted Exams Name of the course Examination Dates Number of Exam Centres May 2nd to 17th 2024 Intermediate: 206410 MAY 2024 Final:116072 Intermediate, Final and International Taxation Assessment Test INTT - AT: 483 (INTT-AT) Foundation:113076 JUNE 2024 Foundation June 20th, 22nd, 24th and 26th 2024 SEPTEMBER Foundation & intermediate September 12th to 23rd 2024 Foundation: 78209 Intermediate: 139646 NOVEMBER Final, International Taxation Assessment Test November 3rd, 5th, 7th, 9th, 11th and 13th Final: 101430 INTT - AT: 339 IRM: 75 INTT -AT and Insurance and Risk Management Technical Examination

(IRM) JANUARY Foundation & intermediate January 11th to 21st Foundation:120609 Intermediate: 178644

(II) CONDUCT OF ADVANCED INTEGRATED COURSE ON INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY AND SOFT SKILLS (ADVANCED ICITSS) - ADV. INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY TEST IN CBT MODE: - During the year 2024-2025, tests were also conducted in Computer Based Mode (CBT) at computer centres for Advanced ICITSS. (XII) INTEGERATION OF ICAI EXAMINATION APPLICATION FORMS WITH SELF SERVICE PORTAL (SSP):In order to facilitate the students, the ICAI Examination Application Forms is integrated with Self Service Portal

(SSP) from the other standalone portal. The integration was done from CA November 2022 examinations and continued in 2024-2025 as well. The integration enables the students to effortlessly fill the CA exam forms at Self Service Portal (SSP) where student details i.e photograph, signature, exemption etc are auto filled and it helps in establishment of eligibility of student to appear for the exam (as per ICAI eligibility criteria for exams) at form filling stage itself. Apart from applying for the exam, student can change his city/group/medium of examination (via Correction window) and can download his/her admit card for the exams as these activities are also integrated in the Self Service Portal (SSP).

(XIII) WEB-INTERFACE ON STUDENTS EXAM LIFE CYCLE MANAGEMENT ICAI embarked on an integrated web-interface called Student Exam Life Cycle Management Project, where CA students using a single user ID and password, can access various examination related services, including application for duplicate marksheets/pass certificates/transcripts, change of centre/medium/group, downloading admit cards, checking results, and applying for verification/seeking certified copies of answer books post result etc. from exam to exam.

(XIV) MCQ BOOKLET TO BE RETAINED BY THE CANDIDATES W.e.f. from CA Examination September 2024 onwards, the candidates have been allowed to take away the MCQ booklet with them.

(XV) DIGITAL MARKING/DIGITAL EVALUATION Continuing the path of 100% Digitalization, approx. 34.13 lakhs answer books were digitally evaluated in the year 2024-2025 which also includes the number of answer books for January 2025 exams. Complete Digitization has resulted in faster evaluation, error-free result and as well as ease and convenience for examiners.

(XVI) ELIGIBILITY TEST FOR EXISTING AND NEW EXAMINERS Mandatory Eligibility test were conducted for new applicant examiners and only those new applicant examiners were allotted examinership assignment who passed the mandatory eligibility test. Similarly, eligibility tests were conducted for existing examiners whose 3 years had lapsed since they passed the eligibility test. Accordingly, 31 Part A tests and 32 Part B tests were conducted in the year 2024-25, whereby 1513 new and existing examiners passed the tests and were empaneled as examiners.

(XVII) DIGITAL WORKSHOP/WEBCAST FOR EXAMINERS & EXAMINATION FUNCTIONARIES Continuing on the path of conducting examiners workshop in Digital Mode, Approx 9800 examiners have attended Digital Workshop in the year 2024-2025. Digital Workshop has resulted in huge cost savings as well as ease and convenience for examiners who otherwise had to travel long distance to attend the physical workshop.

Programme(s) for enhancing the quality & consistency of evaluation of answer books through webcast for examiners were successfully conducted for May/June, September, November 2024 and January 2025 Examinations. This initiative is expected to go a long way in improving the quality of evaluation. Webcast on guidelines for observers, examination centres, Bank of Baroda, Examination Co-ordinators were successfully done for May/June, September, November 2024 and January 2025 Examinations for better coordination in the examination conducting machinery.

(XVIII) OBSERVER WEB PORTAL & MOBILE APP (ANDROID/IOS)/ CENTRE WEB PORTAL A web portal https://observers.icaiexam.icai.org/ is in place for facilitating all activities relating to Observers appointed in examination centres, including registration, hosting of details of assignments allotted, submission of helpful in ensuring collection of sealed packets of correct code meant for the day. A web portal https://centres.icaiexam.icai.org/ is in place for capturing of absentee data online on the day of the exam on a daily basis which is of great help in collating the absentee records.

(XIX) SCANNING OF QR CODE BY CENTRE FUNCTIONARIES: - W.e.f. CA Examination May 2024 Centre Superintendent/his authorized representative is also required to scan QR Code of the sealed packets collected from Bank at the Centre Portal i.e., https://centres.icaiexam.icai.org/qrcodev01. This exercise by the Centre Superintendent or his/her authorized representative is done only after the scanning of the QR code by the Observer which brings in double check on the correctness of the question paper code for the respective day of examination.

5. Disciplinary Directorate The Disciplinary Directorate, the regulatory arm of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), has been established to investigate cases of Professional and/or Other Misconduct alleged against its members. These cases may be received through a Formal Complaint in Form I or via the Information route, as provided under the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 read with The Chartered Accountants (Procedure of Investigations of Professional and Other Misconduct and Conduct of Cases) Rules, 2007. Under the disciplinary framework, the Disciplinary Directorate has a statutory obligation to examine all alleged lapses or irregularities committed by members across India. This mechanism not only helps maintain public confidence and trust in the profession but also sets a benchmark for acceptable professional conduct. While the vast majority of Chartered Accountants continue to offer dedicated and ethical services to society through their expertise, the disciplinary process ensures that the few who stray intentionally or inadvertently are duly corrected.

Following the 2006 amendments to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, ICAI's disciplinary framework was significantly strengthened to expedite the resolution of disciplinary cases. Consequently, the disciplinary mechanism currently operates through two quasi-judicial bodies established under the Chartered Accountants (Amendment) Act, 2006: • Board of Discipline (BoD) under Section 21A • Disciplinary Committee (DC) under Section 21B This structure ensures transparency, enhances stakeholder confidence, and guarantees fair and equitable justice for members facing allegations. To further enhance accountability and expedite justice, additional amendments were made to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 in the year 2022. However, the date from which these revised disciplinary provisions will come into effect is yet to be notified. During the Council year 2024-25, for efficient case management and swift resolution, five Benches of the Disciplinary Committee—Bench I, II, III, IV, and V (handling MNAF cases)—and one Bench of the Board of Discipline were constituted. These benches worked actively on cases under enquiry, including reviewing Prima Facie Opinions formed by the Director (Discipline).

Additionally, the Disciplinary Committee under Section 21D, chaired by the ICAI President, was constituted to address residual legacy cases that are/may be referred back under the old disciplinary mechanism.

(I) Key Initiatives and Achievements • E-Hearings: The Directorate successfully conducted e-hearings with effective participation from members of the BoD and DC as well as parties. These hearings proved highly beneficial by saving time and cost and enabling broader participation by the parties without the burden of travel. The increased disposal rate of cases is a testament to their effectiveness. • Revamped Web Portal: Recognizing the need for modernization, the existing web portal of the Disciplinary Directorate i.e https://disc.icai.org has been comprehensively revamped. The revamped portal now serves as a one-stop destination for all information related to the Disciplinary mechanism. It has been thoughtfully redesigned to highlight key updates such as recent meetings and Orders/Findings passed by the Board of Discipline (BOD) and the Disciplinary Committee (DC) directly on the homepage. The new structure ensures that stakeholders can access relevant information more efficiently. It now aligns with the ICAI's official colour theme for a consistent visual identity. • Capacity Building: To enhance operational efficiency and keep pace with developments in laws and regulations, regular training sessions are conducted for the Directorate's officials and staff. These sessions cover crucial topics such as Corporate Laws, CARO, and Ind AS etc., ensuring that the team remains efficient, competent and updated. • Training on Artificial Intelligence: Embracing the potential of emerging technologies, a specialised three-day training programme on Artificial Intelligence was organized in May 2025 with the help of the 'AI in ICAI Committee'. Tailored to the specific needs of the Disciplinary Directorate, this programme aimed at equipping Directorate's Officers with the knowledge and skills necessary to harness AI tools effectively in disciplinary functions, paving the way for smarter, technology-driven processes. • Resolution of Cases under old mechanism: As of now, all residual cases under the old disciplinary mechanism (Section 21D) have been heard and concluded by the Disciplinary Committee. Furthermore, no cases remain pending before the Council for consideration.

(II) Board of Discipline under Section 21A of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 The Board of Discipline has been constituted by the Council of ICAI under Section 21A of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 so as to look into matters of Professional and Other Misconduct by members falling under First Schedule to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 and/or cases wherein the members are held prima facie NOT Guilty of any misconduct by Director (Discipline). During the period (i.e., 1st April 2024 to 31st May 2025), the Board of Discipline held 29 meetings in 30 sittings at various places, including meetings through physical/video conference. In these meetings, the Board concluded its enquiry in 36 cases, including cases which had been referred to it in previous years. The statistical break-up of the cases decided by the Board of Discipline is given below:

Sl. No.

Particulars Figure a) No. of meetings of the Board of Discipline held during the aforesaid period (Sittings 30) b) Number of Complaint/Information cases considered by the Board of Discipline (under Section 21A) wherein Prima Facie Opinion* of the Director (Discipline) was formed (including cases considered under Rule 6/Rule 12) c) Number of cases (Complaint/Information cases) in which enquiry was completed by the Board of Discipline (including those cases, which were referred to the Board of Discipline during earlier years) d) Number of cases (Complaint/Information) in which punishment has been awarded by the Board of Discipline (including those cases, which were referred to the Board of Discipline during the earlier years. e) Number of miscellaneous matters considered (under Rule 5(6), jurisdiction issue, etc.)

(III) Disciplinary Committee under Section 21B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 The Disciplinary Committee has been constituted by the Council of ICAI under Section 21B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 so as to look into matters of Professional Misconduct by members which fall within the purview of Second Schedule or both First and Second Schedules to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949. During the period (i.e. 1st April, 2024 to 30th May, 2025), Disciplinary Committee (all benches) held 79 meetings including meetings through physical/video conference mode. During the course of the aforesaid meetings, the Committee concluded its enquiry in 225 cases, which included cases referred to it in previous years. The statistical break-up of the cases decided by the Disciplinary Committee is given below:

Particulars No. a) No. of meetings of the Disciplinary Committee held during the aforesaid period b) Number of Complaint/Information cases considered by the Disciplinary Committee (under Section 21B) wherein prima facie opinion of the Director (Discipline) was formed c) Number of cases (Complaint/Information cases) in which enquiry was completed by the Disciplinary Committee* (including those cases, which were referred to the Disciplinary Committee during the earlier years). d) Number of cases (Complaint/Information) in which punishment has been awarded by the Disciplinary Committee* (including those cases, which were referred to the Disciplinary Committee during the earlier years). * Also includes cases closed on account of Honorable SC judgement in TAMC cases

(IV) Disciplinary Committee under Section 21D The Disciplinary Committee functioning under the provisions of Section 21D of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 conducts enquiry and submits its report to the Council in respect of residual cases pending prior to the amendments made in the aforesaid Act in 2006.

Cases dealt with under the Old Disciplinary Mechanism [Section 21D] Since all the residual cases were already heard and concluded by the Disciplinary Committee in 2018, during the period under review, no meeting of this Committee was held. During the aforesaid period, one case referred by the Honorable Supreme Court of India was considered by the Council. After accepting the report of the Disciplinary Committee, in terms of the provisions of Section 21(5) of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 (unamended), the Council decided to recommend to the relevant High Court the removal of the concerned member's name from the Register of Members. Following the consideration of this case, no other report of the Disciplinary Committee remained pending before the Council.

6. TECHNICAL COMMITTEES/BOARDS

6.1 Accounting Standards Board (ASB) The Accounting Standards Board (ASB), established by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) in 1977, is aimed with formulating Accounting Standards to strengthen the quality, consistency and reliability of financial reporting in India. Its primary objectives are to harmonize accounting practices across the country and to align Indian standards with the International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS) issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (IASB). Over time, the ASB has actively revised existing standards and developed new ones to reflect changes in the business environment, economic conditions and regulatory landscape. To facilitate the effective and uniform application of these standards, the ASB also issues implementation guidance, technical notes, and other interpretative materials on a regular basis. Under Section 133 of the Companies Act, 2013, the ICAI, through the ASB, is entrusted with formulating Accounting Standards applicable to companies.

Additionally, the ICAI issues separate Accounting Standards tailored to the needs of non-corporate entities.

The following are the major activities undertaken by the Accounting Standards Board (ASB) during the period under Report:

(I) Financial Reporting Standards:

Amendments to Ind AS notified by MCA • Amendments to Ind AS were notified by the MCA vide Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Amendment Rules, 2024, comprising issuance of new insurance standard (Ind AS 117) and consequential amendments to fourteen (14) Ind AS (Ind AS 104 superseded). • Amendments to Ind AS 116, Leases regarding sale and leaseback transactions were notified by the MCA vide Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Second Amendment Rules, 2024. • Notification for reinstatement of Ind AS 104, Insurance Contracts, by the MCA vide Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Third Amendment Rules, 2024. • Amendments to Ind AS 21 wrt Lack of Exchangeability were notified by the MCA vide Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Amendment Rules, 2025.

Amendments to AS issued by the ICAI • Issued Revised criteria for classification of non-company entities for applicability of Accounting Standards issued by the ICAI. • Amendments to AS 22, Accounting for Taxes on Income arising due to International Tax Reform-Pillar Two Model Rules have been issued by the ICAI for non-company entities and the same are effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after April 1, 2024.

Amendments to the Ind AS and AS on the following subjects have been recommended to NFRA during the period: • Accounting Standards for Limited Liability Partnerships. • Annual Improvements to Ind AS (2024) - Corresponding to Annual Improvements to IFRS Standards Volume 11 issued by the IASB. • Amendments to the Classification and Measurement of Financial Instruments: Amendments to Ind AS 109 and Ind AS 107.

Ongoing Important Projects related to Ind AS: • Formulation of Ind AS 118, Presentation and Disclosure in Financial Statements and Ind AS 119 Subsidiaries without Public Accountability. • Amendments to Ind AS 109 and Ind AS 107-Contracts Referencing Nature-dependent Electricity.

(II) International initiatives Forging long lasting partnership • In order to play an active role in standard setting at global level, comments were submitted on the various Consultative Documents/Exposure Drafts/Discussion Papers/Tentative Agenda Decisions) issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (IASB) and IFRS Interpretations Committee IFRS (IC) and other international bodies. Following are the details of the submissions during the period: ➢ Exposure Draft on International Non-Profit Accounting Guidance (INPAG)- Part 2. ➢ Exposure Draft of International Non-Profit Accounting Guidance (INPAG) - Part 3. ➢ Exposure Draft on Equity Method of Accounting - IAS 28, Investments in Associates and Joint Ventures (revised 202x). ➢ Exposure Draft on Business Combination - Disclosures, Goodwill, and Impairment (Proposed amendments to IFRS 3 and IAS 36). ➢ Exposure Draft on Contracts for Renewable Electricity (Proposed amendments to IFRS 9 and IFRS 7). ➢ Exposure Draft on Climate-related and Other Uncertainties in the Financial Statements - Proposed illustrative examples. ➢ Exposure Draft on Translation to a Hyperinflationary Presentation Currency - Proposed amendments to IAS 21. ➢ Exposure draft of Provisions- Targeted Improvements - Proposed amendments to IAS 37. ➢ Tentative Agenda Decision on Recognition of Revenue from Tuition Fees (IFRS 15). ➢ Tentative Agenda Decision on Guarantees Issued on Obligations of Other Entities. • ICAI representatives actively participated in meetings of various international standard setting forums, such as, the Emerging Economies Group (EEG), International Forum of Accounting Standard Setters (IFASS), and Asian-Oceanian Standard-Setters Group (AOSSG). Also presented relevant agenda items raising Indian concerns.

International Forum of Accounting Standards Setters (IFASS): • IFASS Meeting held on April 17-19, 2024 - Hon'ble Vice President, ICAI presented on "Accounting of Financial Instruments" wherein the issue of accounting of corporate guarantee contracts and issue of Foreign Currency Convertible Bonds (FCCBs) and effects of relevant laws or regulations arising out of Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity were highlighted. • Attended IFASS meeting held on September 24-25, 2024 in London. • Attended IFASS meeting held from March 12-14, 2025, at Naples, Italy.

Asian-Oceanian Standard-Setters Group (AOSSG): • Attended AOSSG Chairs Advisory Committee (CAC) meeting held on March 19, 2025, through virtual mode.

Emerging Economies Group (EEG): • Attended meeting of the Emerging Economies Group (EEG) held on May 28-29, 2024 at Taiyuan, Shanxi, China. • Attended EEG meeting held on December 17-18, 2024 through virtual mode. • Attended Meeting held on May 7-8, 2025, through virtual mode.

Accounting Standards Advisory Forum (ASAF): • ASAF meeting held on September 26-27, 2024 – India being the sub-working group leader on leases attended the session through virtual mode. In this regard, Chairman, ASB, presented implementation and ongoing application issues of IFRS 16, Leases, in AO regions, as part of PIR of IFRS 16 to help the IASB to identify matters to include in the RFI for public consultation.

(III) Building robust relationship with Regulatory Bodies • ASB continued working on various accounting issues referred by various Regulators (such as, Ministry of Corporate Affairs, Reserve Bank of India, Ministry of Power etc.) and, wherever felt appropriate, various accounting issues were taken up with the relevant Regulators.

(IV) Knowledge Dissemination and Capacity Building Initiatives • More than 80 CPE events were organized in collaboration with various Branches and Regional Councils to raise awareness about recent amendments in AS and Ind AS. These initiatives are also aimed at strengthening the capacity-building efforts of different organizations while providing valuable support to the members in the form of Seminars, Webinars Deep dive courses and Certificate Course.

(V) Other Initiatives: • 3rd Accounting Standards Day was celebrated on May 3, 2025 in New Delhi on the theme 'Navigating the Financial Reporting Landscape'. The event was graced by Honorable Chief Minister of Delhi, Smt. Rekha Gupta as the Chief Guest. The event aimed to engage stakeholders and raise awareness about the crucial role of Accounting Standards in the preparation of financial statements, thereby empowering financial reporting of the country. AS Day was observed simultaneously on pan India basis through all five regional Councils and accordingly the event was celebrated at Delhi, Jaipur, Mumbai, Chennai and Kolkata, reaching a wide audience of industry professionals. • A Weekly Accounting Quiz was introduced (earlier done on Monthly basis) serving as an educational tool to simplify accounting and finance concepts. Designed with a gamified approach, it enhances stakeholders' clarity and awareness of various accounting topics, enabling them to learn effectively and deepen their understanding in an engaging manner.

(VI) Publications released • Indian Accounting Standards (Ind AS): Disclosure Checklist (Revised April 2024) • Accounting Standards: Quick Referencer (As on April 01, 2025)

6.2 Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (AASB) Audit plays a critical role in protecting public interest by enhancing accountability and fostering trust in financial reporting, thereby supporting economic growth. Increasing business complexity and expanding global operations have heightened stakeholder expectations of the auditing profession. In response, the ICAI's Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (AASB) develops high-quality Standards on Auditing, Review, Assurance, Quality Control, and Related Services, which set best practices and serve as benchmarks for auditor performance.The Board also issues Guidance Notes on general and industry-specific auditing issues to provide practical support to auditors. These are released under the ICAI Council's authority, except for auditing standards related to companies and Limited Liability Partnerships (LLPs), where the Central Government prescribes standards based on ICAI's recommendations, in consultation with the National Financial Reporting Authority (NFRA). ICAI's auditing standards are harmonized with the International Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (IAASB) to maintain global consistency. To aid effective implementation, the Board publishes Technical Guides, Practice Manuals, Studies, and Implementation Guides, offering members practical tools to navigate complex audit scenarios confidently.

Important achievements:

(I) Representations/ suggestions to Ministries/Regulators • Development of Digital Balance Confirmation Portal The Board has collaborated with the Indian Banks' Association (IBA) to develop a common Portal for balance confirmation in the banking sector ("Digital Balance Confirmation Portal"). ICAI has entered into a tri-party memorandum of understanding with IBA and PSB Alliance Private Limited for this initiative. The beta version of Phase 1 of the Portal was launched at the World Forum of Accountants (WOFA) on 2nd February 2025. The development of the Portal is currently ongoing. • ICAI's Recommendations on Standards on Auditing for Audit of Companies In June 2022, the Board submitted ICAI's recommendations on the Standards on Auditing for audit of companies to NFRA for notification under Section 143(10) of the Companies Act, 2013. At its 18th meeting held on 11th & 12th November 2024, NFRA considered ICAI's recommendations. During this meeting, NFRA finalised 40 Standards and decided to recommend them to the Central Government for notification under the Companies Act, 2013. Subsequently, NFRA submitted its recommendations to the Central Government. • ICAI's Recommendations on Standards on Auditing for Audit of LLPs In August 2024, the Board submitted ICAI's recommendations on the Standards on Auditing for audit of LLPs ("SAs for LLP") to NFRA for notification under Section 34A of the LLP Act, 2008. ICAI recommended that the Standards on Auditing recommended by ICAI to NFRA for audit of companies should apply mutatis mutandis for audit of LLPs. Subsequently, MCA directed ICAI to develop a separate set of SAs for LLPs for notification under Section 34A of the LLP Act, 2008. In response, the Board developed a separate set of SAs for LLPs. The Council of ICAI, at its meting held in May 2025, considered and approved the Board's recommendations on these SAs for LLPs. ICAI's final recommendations on SAs for LLPs will be submitted to NFRA shortly. • Capacity Building Programme for Officers of the C&AG of India ICAI, in collaboration with the Office of the C&AG of India, organized a Capacity Building Programme for officers of the C&AG engaged in the audit of Public Sector Undertakings (PSUs). The programme was conducted at the ICAI's Centre of Excellence in Hyderabad from 5th May, 2025 to 4th June, 2025. The programme covered a range of topics including Indian Accounting Standards, Standards on Auditing, sectorspecific auditing topics, and information technology-related topics. AASB provided support for the auditingrelated topics of the programme. AASB contributed by preparing background material and arranging faculty with subject matter expertise.

(II) Standards issued The Board issued the following Standards on Quality Management (SQM) under the authority of the Council of ICAI: • SQM 1, "Quality Management for Firms that Perform Audits or Reviews of Financial Statements, or Other Assurance or Related Services Engagements". • SQM 2, "Engagement Quality Reviews".

These Standards are effective from April 1, 2025, on a recommendatory basis, and from April 1, 2026, on a mandatory basis. Upon becoming effective, SQM 1 and SQM 2 will replace the existing Standard on Quality Control (SQC 1).

(III) Publications The Board issued the following publications for the benefit of the members at large: • Guidance Note on Audit of Banks 2025 Edition. • Guidance on Non-Compliances Observed by Quality Review Board During Quality Reviews (Volume 2). • Guidance on Non-Compliances Observed by Quality Review Board During Quality Reviews (Volume 3).

(IV) Initiatives for the Members • Seminars/Webinars organized by AASB The Board organized various seminars, conferences, workshops, webcasts on Engagement and Quality Control Standards, bank audit and other auditing aspects for awareness and professional enhancement of the members. 206 Programs were organised which were attended by more than 80,000 participants. • Train the Trainers Programme The Board organized its first "Train the Trainers" programme on seven new/revised Engagement and Quality Management Standards [SQM 1, SQM 2, SA 220(Revised), SA 250(Revised), SA 315(Revised), SA 540(Revised), SRS 4400(Revised)] on 11th & 12th May 2024 at Delhi. The programme is an important initiative of the Board to empower audit faculties, speakers and the members on these standards. • Audit Day Programme The Board organized the first "Audit Day" Programme on 30th August 2024 at Delhi. This programme was organized to highlight the significance of enhancing audit quality and to showcase the role of the Board in establishing high-quality auditing standards. • Bank Audit Expert Panel The Board constituted an online panel of experts to address Bank Branch Audit related queries of the members for the financial year 2024-25. The Panel addressed queries from 1st April 2025 to 15th April 2025.

(V) Contribution at International Platform • Vice-Chairman, AASB for the Council Term 2022-25 attended IAASB meetings held in September 2024 and December 2024 at New York, USA. • Chairperson, AASB attended IESBA, IAASB and Joint JSS meeting held in May 2025 at New York, USA.

6.3 Expert Advisory Committee (EAC) In the current prevailing economic environment, marked by complex transactions and a continually evolving financial reporting framework, accounting professionals are increasingly confronted with challenging situation that require careful interpretation and application of such framework including accounting and auditing principles to novel transactions. This gives rise to the need for credible and well-researched solutions in such situations to uphold the integrity and reliability of financial reporting. The extensively researched and deliberated opinions issued by the Expert Advisory Committee play a pivotal role in supporting the accounting professionals and preparers of financial statements by offering the guidance on the complex accounting situations/transactions. Since its inception, the Committee has been continuously providing opinions on the matters referred to it by the members of the Institute across all industries as well as by the government and regulatory bodies.

(I) Expert Opinions The Committee answers queries received from the members of the ICAI in accordance with Advisory Service Rules. As per Rules, the Committee answers queries relating to accounting and/or auditing principles and does not answer queries which involve only legal interpretation of various enactments. It also does not answer queries which involve professional misconduct or on a matter pending before the Disciplinary Committee of the Institute, any court of law, the Income-tax authorities or any other appropriate department of the Government. These Rules are available on the website of the ICAI, at its link, https://www.icai.org/post/495 or can be obtained from the Institute's Head Office at New Delhi. The opinions issued by the Expert Advisory Committee are based on the specific facts and circumstances provided by the querist, taking into account the relevant laws, statutes, and applicable accounting and auditing principles. Each opinion clearly states the date of its finalisation and should be read considering the developments and/or amendments to laws or standards, that may have occurred after that date.

(II) Opinions finalised during the period The Committee has finalised opinion on following Fifty five (55) queries on diverse accounting issues received from members and a technical accounting issue received from the National Highways Authority of India (NHAI): • Accounting for subsidy receivable under Ind AS framework. • Accounting treatment of cost of land acquired and cost incurred on (III) Compendium of Opinions/Dissemination of knowledge In order to disseminate the accounting guidance contained in the opinions to the CA professionals and other stakeholders, the opinions issued by the Committee are periodically published as a Compendium of Opinions. So far, forty-three volumes of the Compendium of Opinions have been published, which are extensively used and relied on by the professionals. For easy reference, these opinions have also been compiled and hosted in the form of an advanced and user-friendly search application on the website of the ICAI. Some of the opinions on contemporary issues and having general relevance are published in every issue of the Institute's Journal, 'The Chartered Accountant'. Recent opinions of the Committee are also hosted on the website of the Institute.

(IV) Webinars/Seminars With an objective to disseminate knowledge and to create awareness amongst the members and other stakeholders about EAC opinions, the Expert Advisory Committee organised 5 Webinars on 'Recent EAC Opinions' under Ind AS Framework. The Expert Advisory Committee organised its first ever physical seminar on 'Recent EAC Opinions under Ind AS Framework' on May 17, 2024 Mumbai. The seminar was well-received by participants, who appreciated the interactive format, the depth of expertise demonstrated by the speakers, and the practical relevance of the opinions discussed.

6.4 Financial Reporting Review Board (FRRB) The Financial Reporting Review Board (FRRB) is playing a vital role in enhancing the quality of financial reporting and auditing practices prevailing in India. It was constituted by ICAI as a proactive initiative to improve quality of financial reporting practices by the enterprises. The FRRB undertakes the review of the general-purpose financial statements of various enterprises with a view to determine, to the extent possible, compliance with the generally accepted accounting principles in preparation and presentation of financial statements, compliance with the disclosure requirements prescribed by regulatory bodies, statutes/rules and regulations relevant to the enterprise and compliance with the reporting obligations of the enterprise as well as the auditor. The Board comprises of members of the Central Council of the ICAI including Government of India nominee. The Board also had the privilege of the representations from the office of the Comptroller and Auditor General of India (C&AG), Central Board of Direct taxes (CBDT), National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development (NABARD) and Niti Aayog during this period.

Accomplishments of the year

(I) Partner in Nation Building – Supporting Regulators In its endeavour to support regulators as well as to promote transparency and reliability in financial reporting, the FRRB has undertaken following activities during the period: • Submitted presentation to MCA on significant non-compliances observed in 7 cases. • Referred 12 cases to Regulators, where significant non-compliances affecting true and fair view of the financial statements were observed (MCA, SEBI, RBI) • Undertaken review of 11 cases referred by Regulators (SEBI, CAG) and on the basis of other references received, as special cases. • Contribution in C&AG-ICAI Training Program for Audit Officers in Public Sector Undertakings

(PSUs) The ICAI has signed MoU with C&AG to develop focused training programs and knowledge-sharing platforms, aimed at enhancing the professional capabilities of audit officers in Public Sector Undertakings

(PSUs). The primary objective is to sharpen the skill sets of auditing professionals thereby enhancing their efficiency, transparency and accountability. As a part of the collaborative effort, FRRB is supporting the training programs by providing relevant study material based on the non-compliances in financial reporting, as identified by it, which comprises 75 case studies covering 30 Ind ASs. This contribution will ensure that the audit officers are equipped with sound knowledge of applicable standards and best practices, thereby elevating the quality of financial oversight in PSUs.

(II) Review of General-Purpose Financial Statements From April 2024 to May 2025, the Board has completed the review of 92 cases. It includes review of 5 special cases and 87 suo motto cases. The classification of these 92 cases is as follows:

Decision taken by the Board on considered cases Out of these 92 cases, 12 cases have been referred to concerned regulators as well as Director (Discipline) of ICAI for further action, and in 59 cases, Board decided to issue advisory to the auditor of the enterprise.

Cases referred to Director Cases referred to Regulators (MCA, SEBI, RBI) for serious Issued advisories to Cases considered and finalised by the Board (Discipline) of ICAI for further action where serious the auditors non-compliances observed non-compliances observed on companies (out of the cases in (B))
