(III) Publicaion Study on Compliances of Financial Reporting Requirements (Ind AS Framework) – Volume III

(IV) Tech Initiatives FRRB has undertaken the following tech-driven initiatives to enhance its internal operational efficiency: • Introducing Master Document System A Master Document System is being introduced on FRRB Portal to centralize all information pertaining to each case in a single document, which further allows the portal to auto-generate various outputs reports tailored for different stakeholders, eliminating duplication and ensuring consistency across all communications. • Repository of Cases A repository has been created on the FRRB portal containing all cases reviewed by the Board since its inception, with an advanced search tool for quick and efficient information retrieval. • Document Management System FRRB has initiated the implementation of the Document Management System (DMS) to facilitate digitized access of its physical records.

(V) X Handle-FRRB In order to spread awareness among the members regarding the non-compliances observed by FRRB and to give regular updates on the same, 'Did you know?' series is going on at FRRB's X account with 4,524 followers as on date.

During the period, 76 tweets have been posted on the errors observed on the compliance related to Accounting Standards (Ind AS and IGAAP), Standards on Auditing (SA) and Schedule III to the Companies Act 2013.

(VI) Initiatives for the Members Given the closure of financial year and finalization of financial statements in the month of April & May, FRRB undertook the initiative aimed at providing insights to the preparers and auditors of the financial statements with regard to common non-compliances observed by the Board during review of various financial statements, which would help them to improvise/enhance the quality of financial reporting. To achieve this: • FRRB organised Webinar Series on 'Commonly found Non-Compliances of Ind AS and Companies Act, 2013' in the month of April 2024. With over 1700 participants, the program was a resounding success.

FRRB organised Webinars on 'Commonly found Non-Compliances observed in Financial Statements and Auditor's Report', in the month of April and May 2025, facilitating pan-India participation. The webinar received an overwhelming response, with 3,000 participants (approx.) in each webinar. • In addition to the webinar, 2 Awareness Programs on Financial Reporting Practices have been organized at Bhopal and Udupi in the month of August and November 2024 respectively and 16 Awareness Programs on Financial Reporting Practices- Shaping the future of Aduit Excellence have been organised in April- May 2025 across various regions/cities i.e. Hyderabad, Mumbai, Nagpur, Kolkata, Bhubaneswar, Vadodara, Ahmedabad, Rajkot, Ernakulam, Thrissur, Kozhikode, Nashik, Rourkela, Navsari, Thiruvananthapuram and Thane. These programs witnessed robust participation with 2575 attendance in aggregate.

6.5 Internal Audit Standards Board (IASB) The Board of Internal Audit and Management Accounting (BIAMA) of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) is entrusted with the mandate of fostering excellence and advancing the disciplines of internal audit and management accounting. The Board plays a strategic role in formulating standards, issuing technical guidance, and facilitating capacity building through knowledge dissemination, training initiatives, and research-driven activities. The Board seeks to enhance the professional competencies of members while promoting sound governance, effective risk management, and informed decision-making within organizations. Through its initiatives, the Board contributes significantly to strengthening financial discipline, accountability, and value creation across sectors. Further, the Board of Internal Audit and Management Accounting was renamed as

"Internal Audit Standards Board" in March 2025.

(I) Certificate Course on Concurrent Audit of Banks The Board of Internal Audit and Management Accounting of the ICAI is conducting Certificate Course on Concurrent Audit of Banks‖ to enable members to supplement the effort of the banks in carrying out internal check of the transactions and other verifications and compliance with the procedures laid down, improve the effectiveness of concurrent audit system in banks, improve quality and coverage of concurrent audit reports and understand the intricacies of concurrent audit of banks. The Board had organised 15 batches of Certificate Course on Concurrent Audit of Banks in Physical and 4 batches in Virtual mode during the period and around 1007 participants have successfully completed the course.

(II) Certificate Course on Internal Audit Certificate Course on Internal Audit has been completely revamped by Board of Internal Audit and Management Accounting, ICAI with new topics and heavy dose of information technology, being launched in a contemporary format to serve the needs of all stakeholders. The Certificate Course on Internal Audit has been designed to help the members to enhance the expertise of the Chartered Accountant in Internal Audit, Build Governance, Risk and Compliance as one of their core competencies and help certified members to distinguish themselves from others conducting internal audits. The Board had organised 1 batches of Certificate Course on Internal Audit in Physical and 2 batches in Virtual mode during the period and around 139 participants have successfully completed the course.

(III) Post Qualification Course - Diploma on Management and Business Finance (DMBF) The Board is running Post Qualification Course - Diploma on Management and Business Finance (DMBF) to enable the members to gain acumen, expertise and in-depth knowledge in the areas of Management and Business Finance. The Board has conducted 5th batch of the said Course in online mode and as of now, 73 members have successfully completed the Course.

(IV) Programmes, Seminars, Conferences and Webinars on Awareness on Internal Audit With a view to provide platform of dissemination of knowledge among members, the Board has organized 41 webinars on Internal Audit on various topics in virtual mode during the period. Various Following programs have been conducted by the Board in physical mode during the period:

(V) Publications Issued Technical Guide on Risk Based Internal Audit of Non- Banking Financial Company

6.6 Peer Review Board (PRB)

(I) ACTIVITIES/ INITIATIVES:

The Peer Review mechanism was introduced by the Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India

(ICAI) with the setting up of the Peer Review Board in March 2002. The Board is progressing satisfactorily since inception and is continuously providing guidance to the members to enhance the efficiency of assurance services rendered by them. The main objective of Peer Review is to ensure that in carrying out the assurance service assignment, the members of the Institute (a) comply with Technical, Professional and Ethical Standards as applicable including other regulatory requirements thereto and (b) have in place proper systems including documentation thereof, to amply demonstrate the quality of the assurance services. The Peer Review is conducted of a Practice Unit by an independent evaluator known as a Peer Reviewer. • Recognition of Peer Review by Regulators:

The Peer Reviewed firms are given recognition even by the regulators as stated below: - ➢ SEBI, with effect from April 1, 2010, has made it mandatory for the listed entities, that limited review/statutory audit reports submitted to the concerned stock exchanges shall be given only by those auditors who have subjected themselves to peer review process and who hold a valid certificate issued by the 'Peer Review Board' of the Institute. ➢ The Comptroller and Auditor General of India (C & AG) has revised the Policy of Empanelment of CA Firms/LLPs and appointment of auditors of Companies under Section 139(5) and 139(7) of the Companies Act 2013 and of Statutory Corporations/Autonomous Bodies as per the provisions of their respective Acts. A maximum of 25 points allotted to firms which hold a Peer Review Certificate on the date of making an application for empanelment. • Peer Review of Practice Units:

The Peer Review Board has considered and issued 4261 Peer Review Certificates to the Practice Units during the period 01.04.2024-31.05.2025. As on 31.05.2025, a total of 6859 Practice Units held valid Peer Review Certificate.

Also, the Peer Review Board has considered 1486 cases of AQMM Review till now having following details:

AQMM Level of Practice Units No. of Practice Units Level 1 Level 2 Level 3 Level 4 Total

(II) Peer Review Mandate-Roll Out Implementation • Applicability of Phase II of the Peer Review Mandate The second phase of the mandate was earlier applicable w.e.f April 1, 2023 for the Practice Units which propose to undertake Statutory Audit of unlisted public companies having paid-up capital of not less than rupees 500 crores or having annual turnover of not less than rupees 1000 crores or having, in aggregate, outstanding loans, debentures and deposits of not less than rupees 500 crores as on the 31st March of immediately preceding financial year OR Practice units rendering attestation services and having 5 or more partners. The Council initially decided to defer the applicability of the second phase of the mandate by three months to be made effective from July 1, 2023. This was later extended upto 31st March 2024. Then, grace period was allowed for another 3 months. Phase II of the mandate was made applicable from July 1, 2024. • Deferment of Phase III of the Peer Review Mandate The third phase of the mandate was earlier applicable w.e.f April 1, 2024 for the Practice Units which have raised funds from public or banks or financial institutions of over Fifty Crores rupees during the period under review or of any body corporate including trusts which are covered under public interest entities OR Practice units rendering attestation services and having 4 or more partners. The Council initially decided to defer the applicability of the third phase of the mandate by nine months to be made effective from January 1, 2025. This was further extended by six months to be made applicable from July 1, 2025. • Deferment of Phase IV of the Peer Review Mandate The third phase of the mandate was earlier applicable w.e.f April 1, 2025 for the Practice Units which propose to undertake audits of branches of Public Sector Banks OR Practice units rendering attestation services and having 3 or more partners.The Council initially decided to defer the applicability of the fourth phase of the mandate by nine months to be made effective from January 1, 2026. • Clarifications/FAQs on the issues arising out of the Peer Review Mandate Peer Review Board has issued clarifications/FAQs on the various issues arising out of the Peer Review Mandate. These clarifications/FAQs are hosted at http://resource.cdn.icai.org/83571prb67450.pdf.

(III) Training & Empanelment of Peer Reviewers: • Training Programme for Peer Reviewers:

The Board conducts training programmes for members to empanel them as Peer Reviewers with the Board. The training sessions guide them about the methodology of conducting Peer Reviews. During the period from April 1, 2024 till May 31, 2025, the Board has organized 101 Peer Review training programmes through physical mode at various Branches/ Regional Councils across the country and 2 virtual interactive meets with the existing Peer Reviewers. • Online Tests for empanelment of Peer Reviewers:

Online tests for empanelment of Peer Reviewers are being conducted every alternate week for the members who have attended the training and are eligible to become Peer Reviewers. Final Assessment Tests are organized every alternate Fridays. In addition to this, Mock Tests are organized for every Final Tests on each preceding Fridays. These tests are being organised twice every Friday- one in the morning and the other in the afternoon. Accordingly, 58 Final and 54 mock tests are organised in the duration of 01.04.2024-31.05.2025. A total of 1556 members have become Peer Reviewers from 01.04.2024-31.05.2025 constituting total 4404 members empanelled as Peer Reviewer with Peer Review Board till 31st May 2025.

Region wise break-up of Number of Trained Peer Reviewers with the Peer Review Board Region No. of Reviewers WIRC SIRC EIRC CIRC NIRC Total

(IV) Peer Review Portal:

The Peer Review Board is developing a dedicated web portal to streamline and enhance its peer review process.

This innovative platform will centralize all review activities, from submission of application to decision-making.

By leveraging advanced technology, we aim to improve efficiency, transparency and effectiveness of peer review process.

(V) Other Initiatives:

The Board has been making continuous efforts to make the Peer Review mechanism more effective. The level of awareness created during the last 23 years has indeed brought about an overall improvement in the quality of assurance services rendered by the members. A comprehensive all-encompassing approach has been followed to create an enabling eco-system including by: • Developing sufficient and competent resources for review; • Improvising the quality of peer reviews; and • Initiating steps to make increased use of technology in the peer review process so as to effectively overcome capacity constraints.

6.7 Taxation Audits Quality Review Board (TAQRB) ACTIVITIES/ INITIATIVES:

The Taxation Audits Quality Review Board was constituted by the Institute in the year 2018 in order to improve the reporting of compliances under various taxation laws (both Direct as well as Indirect). It is envisaged that the reviews carried out by the Board, will ensure that the members will exercise greater diligence while certifying the various reports prescribed under direct and indirect taxation and in the long-run would improve the overall reporting and certification done by them.

(I) Status of Review of Tax Audit Reports selected:

Since inception, the Board reviewed various Tax Audit reports and based on the review: • Advisories are being issued to members to ensure that such mistakes are not committed again. • Suggestions have been identified to be conveyed to CBDT for changes in the Tax Audit Report e-filing utility. • Suggestions have been identified which was incorporated in the latest edition of Guidance Note on Tax Audit. • Commonly found irregularities/ non- compliances committed while furnishing Tax Audit Reports have been identified for the purpose of creating awareness amongst the members.

Status of Review of Tax Audit Reports selected during the Council Years 2024-25 and 2025-26- The Board during the Council Year 2024-25 selected 123 companies (pertaining to AY 2023-24) for review of their Tax Audit reports on suo motto basis. The Board during the Council Year 2025-26 selected 1000 companies for review of their Tax Audit reports Pertaining to AY 2024-25. The process of downloading of the annual reports has been commenced and letters are being issued to Statutory auditors requesting for sharing the details of the tax auditors.

Initiatives for the Members

(II) Seminars/Webinars/Orientation programme • The Board organised 'Discussion on Union Budget 2024-25' along with Direct Taxes Committee of ICAI and hosted by NIRC on 24th July 2024 at New Delhi. The Programme was graced by Chairman, CBDT as Chief Guest, Joint Secretary (TPL)-I, CBDT, Joint Secretary (TPL)-II, CBDT and Tax Expert. • The Board conducted three webinars from the series- Tax Audit Practice Session to aware the members on the compliances to be followed while conducting Tax Audits. • The orientation program for Technical Reviewers was held on 18th July 2024 at New Delhi in a hybrid mode. Technical Reviewers from NIRC and CIRC attended physically or virtually, while others joined online. The program covered the role of Technical Reviewers, reporting on the TAQRB web portal, and the tax audit review checklist, explained by TAQRB leadership and officials. Many queries raised by Technical Reviewers were addressed.

(III) Regular update on Official X (Twitter) Handle of the BoardThe official Twitter account of the Board i.e., @Taqrbicai is being regularly updated with its 'Tax Audit Tips' series to aware the members about the compliances to be taken care of while conducting and filing the Tax Audit Report.

(IV) TAQRB Web Portal: Driving Automation & EfficiencyTo enhance operational efficiency and streamline processes, the Board has developed a web portal (taqrb.icai.org). This portal serves as a centralized repository for TAQRB reports, minutes, and case followups, ensuring seamless documentation and accessibility. Moreover, the system automates key workflows across various levels (TR, TAQRG, and TAQRB), including case allocation, report submissions, and realtime tracking of progress. By integrating automation, the portal enhances transparency, improves coordination, and accelerates decision-making, reinforcing TAQRB's commitment to efficiency and digital transformation. The steps taken above are expected to improvise the quality of tax audit conducted by the members.

6.8 Direct Taxes Committee (DTC) The Direct Taxes Committee (DTC) of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) is a key committee dedicated to matters related to direct taxation. It actively engages with the Government, the Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT), and other relevant forums by making representations on legislative amendments and pressing issues concerning direct taxes. A primary focus of the Committee is to enhance the knowledge and skills of ICAI members in direct taxation through the publication of new materials, updates to existing resources, and the organization of seminars, webinars, and conferences.

(I) Inputs/Memoranda submitted and interactions with the Central Board of Direct Taxes

(CBDT)/Government departments etc. • ICAI's inputs on the Income-tax Bill, 2025 ➢ Presentation of Preliminary Suggestions on Comprehensive Review of the Income-tax Act, 1961 by the ICAI leadership at the stakeholder's consultation under the chairmanship of the Revenue Secretary, Ministry of Finance on 18th September, 2024 before the Committee set up at the level of CBDT ➢ Submission of ICAI's inputs on Comprehensive Review of the Income-tax Act, 1961 on 24th December, ➢ Presentation of Preliminary Suggestions on the Income-tax Bill, 2025 by the ICAI leadership before the Select Committee for examining the Income-tax Bill, 2025 on 6th March, 2025. ➢ Submission of Memorandum of Suggestions on Income-tax Bill, 2025 on 21st April, 2025. • Preparation and Submission of Pre and Post Budget Memorandum ➢ Pre-budget Memorandum 2024 - Presentation of Pre-budget Suggestions by Chairman, Direct Taxes Committee and Central Council Members before the Chairman, CBDT on 24th April, 2024 and 27th June, 2024 and submission of Pre-budget Memorandum -2024 on 24th April, 2024 and addendum thereto on 10th May, 2024 ➢ Pre-budget Memorandum 2025 - Presentation of Pre-budget Suggestions by Chairman, Direct Taxes Committee on 11th November, 2024 before Chairman, CBDT and submission on Pre-Budget Memorandum – 2025 on 24th December, 2024 ➢ Post-Budget Memorandum 2024 submitted on 6th August, 2024 ➢ Post-Budget Memorandum 2025 submitted on 13th March, 2025 • Submission of inputs for Project Integrated E-filing and CPC (IEC) 3.0 and CPC (TDS) 2.0 ➢ Submission of Inputs on Project Integrated E-filing and CPC (IEC) 3.0 to the Income-tax Department on 13th December, 2024. ➢ Submission of Inputs on CPC (TDS) 2.0 to Income-tax Department on 23rd December, 2024. • ICAI's representative on the Consultative Group on Tax Policy constituted at NITI Aayog A Consultative Group on Tax Policy has been constituted at NITI Aayog in order to support the goal of improving tax policies, tax administration and tax compliance while simplifying processes and reducing litigation. Chairman, Direct Taxes Committee was nominated as the ICAI's representative on the Consultative Group. Three meetings were held and suggestions on mitigating litigation and reducing compliance burden were prepared and submitted to the Consultative Group on Tax Policy constituted at NITI Aayog. • Submission of responses on taxation topic to Joint Working Group constituted by the Department of Revenue to steer mandate under Taxation topic for World Bank's B-Ready Project For outreach, 30-minute slots on B-ready were arranged in events conducted by WIRC and SIRC in the regions. • Submission of ICAI's inputs on Cyprus Income-tax Law ICAI had received a request from High Commission of India, Nicosia (Cyprus), seeking inputs on the ongoing comprehensive tax reform process in Cyprus through revision of the Income-tax Act, 2002, especially relating to the provisions concerning individual and corporate taxation, and the changes that would be beneficial to our stakeholders, members, and their businesses. The Committee submitted ICAI Inputs on the Cyprus Tax Reform through revision of Income Tax Act 2022 on 29th August, 2024.

(II) Representations to CBDT to address concerns relating to tax matters The Committee had made following representations to CBDT for• Extending the last date for linking of PAN with Aadhar to 31.7.2024 • Allowing rebate u/s 87A in respect of income-tax on short-term capital gains chargeable u/s 111A and • Addressing the concerns of eligible assessees, who have filed the original return of income under the old tax regime, in filing revised return after the due date u/s 139(1) under the old tax regime • Addressing concerns in relation to – ➢ the variance in Form No. 10B/10BB and ITR 7 on account of non-updation of Form No.10B/10BB in line with the amendment by the Finance Act, 2023 ➢ restriction of applicability of clarification given in Circular No. 17/2023 dated 9th October 2023 to only A.Y.2023-24 • Resolving the genuine hardship faced while furnishing Tax Audit Report for Assessment Year 2024-2025 • Extending the time limit for updation of UDIN by Chartered Accountants after submission of Audit Report under section 44AB in the e-filing portal upto the due date of filing of return of income i.e., 31st October, 2024/30th November, 2024, as the case may be • Extending the applicability of Safe Harbour Rules to A.Y. 2024-25 and A.Y.2025-26 • Tax exemption/relief on withdrawal from National Savings Scheme (NSS) in light of notifications issued by Ministry of Finance on discontinuation of interest after 1st October 2024 • Addressing the concern on account of receiving intimation under section 139(9) for defective return on account of non-reflection of income appearing in Form 26AS in the return of income filed • Resolving the genuine hardship faced by taxpayers and professionals on account of the technical glitches in return filing on the Income-tax e-filing Portal • Addressing the concerns on account of intimation(s) being sent under section 139(9) for defective return in certain cases • Request to address the concerns of taxpayers while filling Schedule 5A - Information regarding apportionment of income between spouses governed by Portuguese Civil Code in the Income-tax Return • One-time relaxation for taxpayers who have omitted to disclose the required information related to foreign income or assets in the income-tax return • Expansion of PAN 2.0 Project to Serve as a Unified All-Inclusive Identity Card • Extending the due date from 31st March 2025 to 30th June 2025 for submitting correction statements for TDS returns pertaining to periods before 31st March 2019 • Excess tax deducted and deposited by the deductor also to be reflected in Form 26AS of deductee to give credit due • Resolving issues faced by taxpayers while filing online rectification application and concerns on account of delay in giving effect to rectification orders issued by Jurisdictional Assessing Officers and delay in processing returns filed in pursuance of an order under section 119(2)(b)

(III) Publications released • Implementation Guide on Revision in Form No.3CD and Form No.3CEB in March, 2024 • Guidance Note on reports of audits under section 12A/10(23C) of the Income-tax Act, 1961 • Foreign Tax Credit Mechanism & Interplay with Domestic Tax Laws. • Controversies in Taxation of Royalty & Fees for Technical Services- A Study • Background Material for Diploma in International Taxation (Ninth edition) • Background Material for Certificate Course on UAE Corporate Tax (Second edition)

(IV) Conferences/Seminars/Workshops/VCM/Webinars/Refresher Course The Direct Taxes Committee organized 78 Seminars/Conferences and 32 VCMs/Webcasts on topics related to Direct Taxes i.e. Overview of the Income-tax Bill, Recent Judicial Pronouncements of Honourable Supreme Court and High Court 2025, Taxation of Trusts / NGOs including recent amendments, Deep Dive into Union Budget 2025-26, the futures of Transfer pricing: Impact of Global & Domestic tax reforms, Tax Implications of Employee Stock Option Plans, Concept of Residency and POEM, Tax Audit Compliance Requirements - A Panoramic View, Introduction and Intricacies of Foreign Tax Credit, Interesting issues in Capital Gains etc. during the period.

6.9 Sustainability Reporting Standards Board (SRSB) The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) has a strong tradition of service to the country towards sustainable as well as economic development. ICAI strives to bring sustainability reporting and thinking within mainstream business practices both in the public and private sectors. ICAI through SRSB is taking proactive steps to not only identify and develop opportunities for Chartered Accountants in Sustainability domain but also to enhance knowledge, upskill and train members by conducting workshops, seminars, and certificate course, publish technical literature on various important topics within the sustainability domain. Further, in its endeavor to promote sustainability ecosystem in the country which would lead to greener low-carbon economy, the Institute is working on two crucial aspects. Firstly, to promote compliance with existing sustainability related disclosure requirements.

Secondly, to strengthen assurance framework to build a solid bridge between sustainability related risks and corporate reporting.

(I) Important Events ICAI Sustainability Reporting Awards 2023-24 The ICAI Sustainability Reporting Awards 2023-24, held on February 1, 2025, at Yashobhoomi, New Delhi, recognized excellence in Business Responsibility and Sustainability Reporting across Large-Cap, Mid-Cap, SmallCap, and Voluntary Disclosure categories, segmented by Manufacturing and Service sectors. The awards highlighted ICAI's dedication to transparency and sustainability aligned with the UN SDGs. The nomination process began on October 15, 2024, receiving 152 submissions, with 151 eligible entries reviewed by a Shield Panel Committee on January 11, 2025. The top 28 nominations were then evaluated by a distinguished Jury at a meet held on January 17, 2025, in Mumbai.

The Jury finalized the awardees under various categories of awards for ICAI Sustainability Reporting Awards 2324 as mentioned below: • Large Cap Manufacturing Sector- Marico Limited Service Sector- Persistent Systems Limited; GAIL (India) Limited • Mid Cap Manufacturing Sector- Amara Raja Energy & Mobility Limited Service Sector- Aavas Financiers Limited • Small Cap Manufacturing Sector- Rallis India Limited Service Sector- Sasken Technologies Limited • Voluntary Disclosures Manufacturing Sector- Concord Biotech Limited Service Sector- Bajaj Allianz Life Insurance Company Limited

(II) Programs organized during the year • National Conference of CAs on Sustainability and Ethics in Accounting: Building a Better Tomorrow was held on August 22-23, 2024, at IMS College, Ghaziabad. The event featured eminent speakers covering topics such as sustainable capital markets, tax evasion, ESG reporting, GST for green growth, automation in financial and sustainability data, breaking growth barriers, and work-life balance. The program was attended by 231 members. • Seminars/Webinars The Board conducted 10 Seminars on "Sustainability Reporting and Social Stock Exchange, The Most Happening Theme Around the World" -Comprehensive Program Focused on Understanding ESG Principles and BRSR, "Social Audit - Building Trust and Driving Impact: The Role of Social Audits in Sustainable Development", "ESG – The Most Happening Theme Around the World a Comprehensive ESG & BRSR Program" etc. The Board also conducted various webinars. • Inputs provided to the Regulators and others on sustainability related matters: ➢ Inputs regarding the Unified Framework for Social Impact Assessment Standards were submitted to the Subgroup of SSEAC (Social Stock Exchange Advisory Committee). 4 meetings were conducted with the Subgroup of SSEAC on 16th March, 3rd April, 10th April, and 16th April, 2024, These meetings aimed to discuss the changes and reviews made on behalf of ICAI. ➢ A Study Group was formed to bring out a Guidance Document for Assurers on BRSR Sustainability Information for the year 2023-24. 2 Group meetings were held on 17th and 18th April, 2024. The meeting on 18 April, 2024 was attended by both assurers and prepares of BRSR reports. ➢ Provided Inputs on Proposed International Standard on Sustainability Assurance (ISSA)-5000, General Requirements for Sustainability Assurance Engagements for meeting of International Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (IAASB) of IFAC held on 18-21 June, 2024 in Madrid, Spain. ➢ Submitted inputs on the SEBI Consultation Paper on the Recommendations of the Expert Committee for Facilitating Ease of Doing Business with respect to Business Responsibility and Sustainability Report

(BRSR). ➢ Provided inputs to the Ministry of Corporate Affairs on IFRS S2 related to the Climate Related Financial Disclosure. ➢ Provided inputs to the Ministry of Corporate Affairs on IFRS S2 related to the Climate Related Financial Disclosure. ➢ Submitted the inputs on the SEBI Consultation Paper on the Recommendations of the Expert Committee for Facilitating Ease of Doing Business with respect to Business Responsibility and Sustainability Report (BRSR). ➢ Submitted the inputs on the SEBI Consultation Paper on Expanding the Scope of Sustainable Finance Framework. The inputs were compiled in response to the above paper issued by SEBI. The comments were finalized in the 33rd Board Meeting and online comments were submitted to SEBI on 6th September, 2024. ➢ A Roundtable Meet with stakeholders was done to get their views on the aspects such as Value Chain, Green Credits, and the substitutions of the term Assurance as mentioned in consultation Paper on the Recommendations of the Expert Committee for Facilitating Ease of Doing Business concerning the Business Responsibility and Sustainability Report (BRSR). The inputs were compiled in response to the above paper issued by SEBI. The comments were finalized in the 31st Board Meeting and online comments were submitted to SEBI on June 12, 2024.

(III)ICAI#75 Green Mahotsav launched on 75th Anniversary of ICAI Marking its 75th anniversary, ICAI, through SRSB and PRC, launched a major environmental initiative to plant 100,000 trees as part of the India Van Mahotsav and the PM's 'Ek Ped Ma Ke Naam' campaign. This effort underscores the commitment to environmental conservation and individual responsibility. So far, 22 Branches have planted 8,104 trees, and 557 members have planted 2,648 trees across India. 557 Certificates of appreciation have been issued to participating members.

(IV) Physical Certificate Course on Sustainability and BRSR • The first batch was held in Ernakulam (December 08, 14, 15, 21, and 22, 2024), while the second batch was held in Bengaluru (December 13, 14, 20, 21, and 27, 2024).

(V) Online Certificate Course on Sustainability and BRSR • The Board has successfully completed the 26 batches and 27th batch is ongoing of online Certificate Course of Sustainability and BRSR and around 2394 participants have enrolled for this course till date.
