9.14 Tender Monitoring Directorate (TMD) In order to monitor, analyze and streamline tendering for professional services of Chartered Accountants, a Directorate namely "Tender Monitoring Directorate" was constituted by the Council during the year 2023-24. The Directorate has been endeavouring to streamline the process of tendering and to monitor the bids quoted by CAs in all the permissible tenders and analyse them. The Directorate further refers the deviations at appropriate level.

Major activities of the Directorate The Directorate has its own Portal for tenders on professional services – www.tmdicai.org. which offers the following features: • Tender opportunities • Latest information for members • Council Guidelines on tenders • Details of minimum recommended scale of fees • Format of cost sheet to be submitted by members • Extract from relevant provisions of Code of Ethics • Representations made by Directorate to the tenderers • Answers to Frequently Asked Questions • Grievance posting mechanism, with or without disclosing identity. • Fee calculator for the ease of calculating fee for audit and other assignments.

Representations are being made to tenderers where minimum fees is not prescribed in respect of audit & attestation services, or where eligibility conditions appear to be unreasonably restrictive or where the services tendered create

"independence" issues. During the functional year 2024-25, 174 such representations were sent to the tendering organisations. During the functional year 2025-26 upto 30th May 2025, so far 51 representations have been sent.

TMD also collects tenders for professional services from all over India and hosts the same in its TMD Portal.

During the functional year 2024-25, 1,313 tenders were studied, out of which 1,156 tenders were hosted in the TMD portal as these were found appropriate in all respects. During the current functional year upto 30TH May 2025, so far 617 tenders have been studied, out of which 564 tenders have been hosted in the TMD portal.

Bids submitted by members are studied and cost sheets are called for wherever it is felt that the bid is for low amount. During the functional year 2024-25, notices were sent to 311 CA firms in respect of 56 tenders. During the current functional year 2025-26, so far notices have been sent to 73 CA firms in respect of 13 tenders. During the year 2024-25, cases of 37 CA firms have been referred to Director (Discipline) for investigation. Peer review has been ordered in respect of 56 CA firms. Cases relating to 129 CA firms have been closed as the cost sheet was found to be satisfactory. Examination of rest of the cases is on. During the last functional year and the current one, representations were sent to Chief Secretaries and Finance Secretaries of all States and Union Territories requesting them, inter-alia, to ensure fairness in tendering process by the organisations under their control. Pursuant to the same, following meetings were held with: • Director, Ministry of Finance, Government of India, New Delhi on 17th May 2024. • Secretary, Ministry of Electronics & Information Technology, Government of India, New Delhi on 22nd May

2024. • Additional Chief Secretary, Government of Karnataka, Bengaluru on 12th June 2024. • Finance Secretary, Government of Jharkhand, Ranchi on 13th June 2024. • Finance Secretary, Government of Chhatisgarh, Raipur on 18th June 2024. • Principal Secretary (Finance), Government of Goa on 11th July 2024. • Deputy Secretary to the Government of Assam on 23rd July 2024 at Finance Department, Janata Bhawan, Dispur, Guwahati - 06, Assam – in the presence of Chairman Guwahati Branch of EIRC of ICAI, Vice Chairperson Guwahati Branch of EIRC of ICAI, Secretary, Guwahati Branch of EIRC of ICAI and Secretary, TMD. • Chief Executive Officer, Government e Marketplace (GeM), on 06th August 2024 at Office of the CEO GeM, 2nd Floor, Jeevan Tara Building, Sansad Marg, Near Patel Chowk, New Delhi-110001. • Additional Secretary, Finance Department to the Government of Madhya Pradesh on 23rd August 2024 at Finance Department, Vallabh Bhawan, Bhopal with present of Chairperson of Bhopal Branch, CICASA Chairperson, Bhopal and Branch Staff.

The TMD along with Committee for Member in Practice (CMP), ICAI has come out with fee calculator for reference by both tendering organisations and CA bidders. The said application was launched by the Council at its meeting held in July 2024. This fee calculator is based on the minimum recommended scale of fees issued by the Council in February 2020. The same can be accessed at https://tmdicai.org/fees-calculator.php.

9.15 UDIN Directorate In today's evolving business and regulatory environment, the Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN), introduced by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), holds critical importance. It acts as a safeguard against the circulation of fraudulent financial documents by assigning a unique identifier to every certificate, audit report, and attestation issued by practicing chartered accountants. This mandatory requirement enables online verification of documents, thereby ensuring their authenticity and legitimacy. UDIN significantly enhances trust and transparency in financial reporting. It enables stakeholders—such as investors, regulators, and government bodies—to swiftly validate certified documents, thereby mitigating risks associated with misinformation or forgery. By enforcing accountability, UDIN promotes adherence to ethical practices and auditing standards, reinforcing a culture of professionalism and responsibility among chartered accountants.

The system has witnessed widespread adoption and has become an integral part of professional practices. It also streamlines regulatory processes by offering a standardized, technology-driven mechanism for document verification. This digital approach not only improves efficiency in audits and compliance checks but also aligns with the broader movement toward digital governance and transparency. As businesses navigate increasingly complex compliance landscapes, UDIN stands out as a vital tool for upholding the integrity of financial reporting.

It fosters stakeholder confidence, supports good governance, and contributes to a more robust and transparent economic ecosystem. In essence, UDIN is a transformative initiative that underscores ICAI's commitment to strengthening the credibility of financial reporting in India. By leveraging technology to deter fraud and ensure document authenticity, UDIN plays a pivotal role in promoting sustainable business growth and regulatory confidence.

(I) UDIN - A decision making parameter for the Regulators, Banks and Other Stakeholders • On 30th May 2025, ICAI showcased the UDIN system before a visiting delegation from Her Majesty's Revenue and Customs (HMRC), UK, at the Centre for Taxation and Finance, New Delhi. This knowledgesharing engagement, held under the collaborative "Risking of Agents" project with the Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT), reflected growing global interest in UDIN as a best practice tool. • In recognition of its effectiveness, the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (MeitY) acknowledged UDIN as a best practice and proposed its integration with the upcoming "Entity-Locker" platform a secure digital repository for storing and sharing documents of large entities, MSMEs, trusts, and societies. This initiative aims to streamline document management and interactions with banks, regulators, and other authorities. MeitY appreciated the role of UDIN in promoting trust and efficiency in digital governance. To enable real-time verification of CA-certified documents, MeitY approached the UDIN Directorate for API integration. Two meetings were held on 30th October 2024 and 17th March 2025 to discuss specific requirements and explore seamless API-based integration within the Entity-Locker framework, strengthening the credibility and traceability of documents in digital ecosystems. • The Director General of Supply and Transport, Indian Army, Ministry of Defence (MoD) approached the UDIN Directorate to provide API integration for the verification of UDIN on a real-time basis. A meeting was held on 23rd December 2024, to understand their specific requirements and to discuss the possibility of incorporating an API for real-time verification of UDINs. • The Chhattisgarh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (CG-RERA) approached the UDIN Directorate to provide an API for enabling real-time verification of UDINs through their system. Following several meetings with CG-RERA and subsequent approval from the competent authority, a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) was signed between ICAI and CG-RERA on 15th October, 2024. This collaboration aims to strengthen transparency and streamline document authentication processes. • Orisha Real Estate Regulatory Authority (ORERA) operates on a digital platform aimed at minimizing physical interactions and is currently upgrading to a new system, ORERA 2.0. Certificates and approvals issued by Chartered Accountants often require independent verification. To facilitate this, Odisha-RERA sought collaboration with the UDIN Directorate to integrate their updated system (ORERA 2.0) with the UDIN portal for real-time UDIN verification through API. A meeting with the Odisha Real Estate Regulatory Authority (ORERA) was held on 18th September 2024 to understand their specific requirements and explore the integration of an API for seamless and real-time verification of UDINs. • The Comptroller and Auditor General of India (C&AG) used the UDINs as one of the elements for the purpose of empanelment of CA firms/LLPs by them for the first time in year 2023-24. C&AG provided the following scoring mechanism for empanelment of CA Firms/LLPs based on UDIN data. "For the year 20252026, points will be awarded on the basis of UDINs generated on ICAI portal during the years 2020-2021, 2021-2022, 2022-2023 and 2023-2024. This will eventually be scaled to 5 years by 2026-2027 by gradually adding one year on annual basis".

(I) UDIN Logo - official visual identity for the Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN) UDIN Logo Overview:

The UDIN initiative has been widely accepted in India for enhancing transparency and professionalism in accounting. To reinforce its identity, ICAI launched a distinctive UDIN logo on February 10, 2025, symbolizing authenticity, integrity, and trust. The design features "CA" within "ICAI," representing Chartered Accountants as pillars of financial prudence, and a tricolor thumbprint reflecting Indian roots and the unique trust behind each UDIN-certified document. The logo provides a clear identity to certified documents, serves as a seal of authentication, and symbolizes the permanent, trustworthy nature of UDIN, strengthening credibility in financial reporting.

(II) Expression of Interest for Adoption of UDIN: The Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN) continues to gain international recognition, with professional bodies from other countries expressing interest in its adoption. • A meeting with the delegation from the Institute of Certified Public Accountants of Kenya (ICPAK) was held on 3rd February 2025 to discuss the process, framework, and protocols for implementing UDIN in Kenya. • Similarly, the National Board of Accountants and Auditors (NBAA), Tanzania expressed keen interest in adopting the UDIN framework. Delegations from NBAA held meetings with the UDIN Directorate in February and April 2023 to explore the implementation strategy. It is a matter of pride that NBAA acknowledged the ICAI's initiative and conveyed their appreciation through an official letter.

(III) Initiatives for the Members • On 11th October 2024, the "Gujarat Public Trust Act, 2011" was added to the dropdown menu of statutes under the "Under Act / Law / Statute / Regulation" section in the "Audit and Assurance Functions" category on the UDIN Portal. This enabled practicing Chartered Accountants to select the statute while generating UDINs for relevant certifications and audit functions. Subsequently, the statute was renamed as the "Gujarat Public Trusts Act, 1950" with effect from 8th May 2025, and the dropdown on the portal was duly updated to reflect the revised title. • A webinar on "Preliminary Insights on UDIN for Bank Audits" was organized on April 7th, 2025, to benefit our esteemed members. The session provided valuable insights into the application and significance of the UDIN (Unique Document Identification Number) in bank audits. • Based on requests received from members, Financial Years 2007–12 have been added in the dropdown for UDIN generation for Form 26A/27BA under the GST Tax Category on the UDIN Portal with effect from 21st March 2025. • It has been decided to change the data type for capturing figures and values from alphanumeric to numeric across all UDIN categories—Certificate, GST & Tax Audit, and Audit & Assurance Functions—in a phased manner. The implementation began with the Audit & Assurance Functions category, which went live on December 6, 2024, followed by GST & Tax Audit on December 21, 2024, and Certificates on December 28,

2024. This change aims to ensure uniformity and consistency in the numerical data entered by members during UDIN generation.

(IV) Impact of UDIN The Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN) system, introduced by ICAI, has significantly enhanced transparency, authenticity, and reliability in professional documentation by preventing forgery and misuse of Chartered Accountants' certifications. Each certified document receives a unique ID, enabling easy verification and drastically reducing counterfeit instances. UDIN has streamlined regulatory compliance, allowing authorities to efficiently verify documents and reduce fraud, thus fostering a more accountable and ethical professional environment. As of June 10, 2025, over 1.53 lakh CAs are registered on the UDIN portal, with more than 8.62 crore UDINs generated across audits, certificates, and tax audits, and over 3.69 crore UDINs verified by regulators, banks, and others.

(V) Publications • Booklet for Members on Bank Audit • UDIN Report (2024–25) and revised FAQs on Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN) (Revised 2025).

10. Students Related Boards/Committee

10.1 BOARD OF STUDIES - ACADEMIC The Board of Studies (Academic) plays an instrumental role in shaping the careers of aspiring CA students by providing them a comprehensive package of educational resources, guiding them at every stage of their academic journey and mentoring them in their goal of becoming a qualified Chartered Accountant. In its endeavour to provide a strong foundation of professional knowledge and professional values, the BoS(A) provides its students quality Study Materials, Revisionary Test Papers, Mock Test Papers, Suggested Answers for each subject of the CA course and also provides Saransh- Last Mile Referencer and Case Scenario Booklets. Live Virtual Classes, ICAI Digital Learning Campus and MCQ Paper Practice Assessment Dashboard are some of the digital initiatives of BoS(A) to facilitate student learning and preparation for their examinations.

(I) Initiatives for Students Study Materials of CA Course Curriculum Study materials help students to develop a strong conceptual base. ·They give students the parameters within which they have to study. They provide detailed explanations of topics, accompanied by ample illustrations.

In the year 2024-2025, study materials of all the subjects across all the levels of CA course curriculum have been revised and released as follows:

LEVEL EDITION Foundation August, 2024 Intermediate July, 2024 Final November, 2024 Multiple-Choice Questions (MCQ) Paper Practice Assessment Dashboard To provide hands-on practice for MCQ-based assessments, the Board of Studies (Academic) has launched the MCQ Paper Practice Assessment Dashboard on the BOS Knowledge Portal and ICAI-BOS Mobile App. This user-friendly tool helps CA students at all levels—Foundation, Intermediate, and Final—self-assess their progress and deepen their understanding of each chapter. It offers a combination of MCQs and case scenario - based questions, with options to select difficulty levels (Simple, Medium, Difficult), and allows students to attempt 20 MCQs in 20 minutes, with extra time reflected in performance reports. The dashboard also tracks paper-wise and chapter-wise performance, with regularly updated content to support continuous preparation.

These lectures are also available at ICAI CA Tube https://www.youtube.com/@theicai/playlists The Board of Studies (Academic) offers free Live Virtual Classes for CA students at all levels, accessible live or as recorded lectures on any device. The sessions, led by expert faculty, come with unlimited access, exam-focused content, notes, assignments, MCQs, and real-time doubt resolution, ensuring comprehensive and flexible learning support.

Featuring experienced faculty and subject experts, these webinars cover study techniques, time management, exam patterns, and tips for tackling challenging topics. They also address common student concerns and aim to motivate and prepare students for success. Two webinar series are held before each examination across all levels. For the 2024-2025 year, BOS (A) conducted 60 webinars for three Foundation and Intermediate exams, and 24 webinars for two Final exams.

Saransh - Last Mile Referencer

"Saransh" is a comprehensive booklet featuring diagrams, flow charts, and tables summarizing subject topics presented in a visually appealing and easy-to-understand format. It serves as a one-stop repository, consolidating significant concepts for quick reference and last-minute revision before exams. In the year 2024-2025, BOS (A) has released twelve subject-wise Saransh booklets for all the subjects of Foundation and Intermediate level of CA course curriculum.

Model Test Paper Booklet Model Test Paper is a booklet which act as a valuable question bank, containing multiple Test Papers. The papers include a variety of question types, helping students familiarize themselves with different ways questions may be framed in the actual exam. The papers provide insight into the structure and format of the ICAI exams. In the year 2024-2025, BOS (A) has released in total 18 booklets for various subjects of ICAI course curriculum.

Case Scenario Booklet The Case Scenario Booklet is designed to enhance students' analytical thinking and application-based learning. The booklet also helps students better prepare for case-based and integrated questions in the examinations. Overall, it fosters a deeper appreciation of the subject matter, promotes interlinking of concepts, and helps bridge the gap between academic learning and real-world expectations. In the year 20242025, BOS (A) has released in total 14 booklets for all subjects of Intermediate and Final level where 30% of the assessment is in the form of case scenario based MCQs.

Launch of ICAI Digital Learning Campus Portal: Self-Paced Online Modules (SPOM) The Board of Studies of ICAI launched the ICAI Digital Learning Campus Portal on 7th May 2024, aligning with the New Scheme of Education and Training. The portal supports Self-Paced Online Modules (SPOM), requiring 40 learning hours each for SET A (Corporate & Economic Laws) and SET B (Strategic Cost & Performance Management), and 20 hours each for SET C and SET D, can choose one paper from 10 Papers of SET C and one paper from 4 papers of SET D, available on the ICAI Digital Learning Campus Portal at https://lms.icai.org/login The Self-Paced Online Modules (SPOM) are exclusively accessible to Final Course students, offering them the flexibility to learn at their own pace, anytime and anywhere. These modules are available at no additional cost. Students can monitor their learning progress through a ICAI Digital Learning Campus dashboard and a proctored learning environment ensures the integrity and credibility of the courses.The current Learning statistics of Self-Paced Online Module Learning completed by the students as on 31st May 2025, are as under:

Set Name Paper Name Completed In-Progress Set A: Self-Paced Online Module Corporate and Economic Laws 1,84,093 25,342 Set B: Self-Paced Online Module Strategic Cost & Performance Management 1,57,154 21,848 Set C: Self-Paced Online Module Paper 1 to 10 23,993 23215 Set D: Self-Paced Online Module Paper 1 to 4 20424 11340 Study Materials for Select Subjects under MRA/MOUs with various Foreign Accounting Bodies ICAI is endeavoring to provide mobility to its members by having the ICAI qualification recognized in different parts of the world. Accounting bodies worldwide mutually recognize the qualification of ICAI for the benefit of members at either ends. Through these agreements, members of one institute can gain membership in the other by passing a limited number of bridging examination papers and fulfilling specific criteria (e.g., good standing, no disciplinary issues, original qualification through examination). For those bridging examination papers, BOS(A) provides study materials as per the MRA/MOUs entered by ICAI with various Foreign Accounting Bodies i.e CPA (AUSTRALIA), CAP MALAYSIAN, CPA (CANADA), CPA (IRELAND), ICAEW, ICAN, SAICA, AUSTRALIA AND NEW ZEALAND.

Study Materials for Select Subjects under Overseas Citizen of India (OCI) Scheme Regulation 53C. Enrolment of Overseas Citizen of India Professional Accountants was inserted in the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988 vide Notification No.1-CA(7)/154/2014 dated 22nd July 2014. As per Regulation 53C(1), an Overseas Citizen of India holding professional accountancy qualification shall be recognised as a member of the Institute on completion of such examination, training and other modules as listed out in Schedule 'B' to the regulations. Schedule B contains the Scheme for enrolment of Overseas Citizen of India (OCI) Professional Accountants. As per this Scheme, to be eligible for membership of the Institute, an OCI having chartered accountant or certified public accountant qualification is required to pass the First Level Test and the Second Level Test and comply with the other provisions of the Scheme. Accordingly, BOS (A) has webhosted the study materials of following subjects:

FIRST LEVEL TEST Section A : Fundamentals of Income-tax (50 Marks) Section B: Mercantile Laws (50 Marks) Section D: General Economics (50 marks) Section E: Quantitative Aptitude (50 marks) SECOND LEVEL TEST Paper 1 : Taxation (Direct & Indirect Taxes) Paper 3 : Corporate and Allied Laws Board of Studies Twitter Handle The Board took an important step in enhancing communication by establishing its official Twitter handle, accessible at https://twitter.com/bosaicai, starting from 25th July, 2023. This platform serves as an avenue to keep our students, members, and other stakeholders informed about the latest initiatives of the BoS(A). As on date BOS Twitter handle is having 31,000+ engaged followers and academic and administrative queries are being addressed by support team.

ICAI-BOS Mobile App and BoS Knowledge Portal Users ICAI-BOS Mobile App and BoS Knowledge Portal serve as a holistic platform, offering a comprehensive package of educational resources to our students. 7,71, 688 students are utilizing the Mobile App & BOS Knowledge Portal.Till now, the BoS(A) Faculty have answered 31,006 subject-specific queries of students.

Moreover, students can track and view their past queries in ICAI BOS Mobile App. Students can download Android and iOS versions of this mobile app from Google and Apple Play Stores.

(II) Programmes organized for Students Charcha Ka Vishay: CA Pariksha – Let's Talk: The CA Exam The Board of Studies (Academic) organized an interactive session titled "Charcha Ka Vishay: CA Pariksha – Let's Talk: The CA Exam" on 25th April 2025 at ICAI Bhawan, New Delhi. The event saw active participation from around 200 students on-site and over 4,200 students joining online. The session aimed to address student queries ahead of the May 2025 Exams, focusing on preparation strategies, stress management, and effective use of ICAI resources.

Launch of the Induction Programme for CA Foundation Students The Board of Studies (Academic) launched its first-ever Structured Induction Programme for newly registered Foundation students on 23rd May 2025, conducted in virtual mode. The programme aimed to familiarize students with ICAI's legacy, structure, and available resources across various departments, including BoS (Academic & Operations), Examination, Publications, Members & Students Services, and international committees. It also introduced students to academic and administrative support systems, mentoring, career guidance, and global opportunities in the CA profession. Over 10,000 students from across India participated enthusiastically.

(III) Initiative for Members and Students Revised Practical Training Guide – A Pillar of Professional Learning in the CA Course The Revised Practical Training Guide, developed by the Board of Studies (Academic), ICAI, is a comprehensive resource aligned with the New Scheme of Education and Training effective from 1st July

2023. It reflects updated provisions of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988 and provides structured guidance for principals and students. The Guide is organized into five chapters covering regulatory aspects, training structure, monitoring mechanisms and industrial training, complemented by six annexures with essential formats and serves as both a regulatory handbook and a practical roadmap for a well-rounded and relevant training experience.

(IV) Interactions with Members and Students Various sessions for members and students across India have been conducted in physical or virtual mode on the areas like passing the CA examination and career growth in CA profession, etc. The members who expressed their interest to be faculty in various courses conducted by BoS were guided of the process. Board of Studies has organized 54 sessions in physical / virtual mode to have interaction with students and members. The sessions for students were emphasized on How to crack and pass CA exam.

(V) Towards Partner in Nation Building Global Education Summit on Commerce and Accountancy 2024 (GES-CA 2024) The Board of Studies organised Global Education Summit on Commerce & Accountancy (GES-CA 2024) in New Delhi on 5th and 6th July 2024, themed "Manthan — A Catalyst for Academic Growth." The summit focused on digital innovations transforming education in accounting, auditing, costing, finance, and commerce. The summit was inaugurated by Hon'ble Minister of State for Corporate Affairs and Road Transport & Highways. The event brought together ICAI leaders, Vice Chancellors, Chairmen, Directors, and faculty from premier Indian universities and colleges. Key discussions covered topics such as digital transformation and tax challenges, inclusive education, sustainability, the evolving role of Chartered Accountants, education reform, skill development, AI and security, mindfulness in education, and global commerce complexities including BEPS and OECD guidelines.

Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) with Universities/Institutions ICAI has signed Memorandums of Understanding (MoUs) with 80 State and Central universities as of 31st May 2025, aiming to promote academic cooperation in undergraduate, postgraduate, and diploma commerce courses. These partnerships focus on developing collaborative programs to unify and modernize the curriculum of Commerce and Accountancy education across India, enhancing synergy between ICAI and educational institutions.

Recognitions for CA Course- Pursuing Ph. D Programme 142 Indian Universities, 7 IIMs and 2 IITs (Madras & Bombay), i.e., in total 151 institutions (WRO-31, SRO39, ERO-19, CRO-41 and NRO-21) in the country have recognized Chartered Accountancy qualification for the specific purpose of registration to Ph.D. programme of respective universities.

Virtual Faculty Development Program (FDP) Series for the Faculty of Universities/Colleges/Accredited Institutions The Board of Studies had organized a series of Virtual Faculty Development Programmes (FDP) for the faculties of universities, colleges, and accredited institutions on Technology 4.0 and Accounting: Use of Blockchain and Artificial Intelligence, Budget: Its Interpretation and Implications, Fintech innovation:

Implication for Financial Management etc for the Faculty of Universities/Colleges/Accredited Institutions.

Joint Seminars/FDPs organized by BoS with University/College BOS organised six Joint Seminars/Faculty Development programmes with University/College on New Age Technology and Its Impact on Financial Domain, Innovation and Critical thinking, Career Opportunities in Finance and Accountancy etc.

Initiatives for stakeholders of SAFA Member Organizations The Board of Studies, in collaboration with the SAFA Committee on Education, Training, and CPD, hosted seven insightful webinars focused on professional growth and adaptability. Topics included International Education Standards (IES), emotional intelligence in leadership, emerging skills such as data analytics and sustainability reporting, and the importance of ethics in a tech-driven world. To encourage student engagement and talent, the SAFA Quiz and Public Speaking Contest 2024 was held on 30th April 2025 in Colombo, Sri Lanka, offering a platform for students from SAFA member bodies to showcase their skills and knowledge. Representing ICAI, winners of the CA Students Talent Search 2024 (Elocution and Quiz Contest) participated in the event. Notably, two ICAI participants secured the 1 st Runner-up position in the Public Speaking Contest, bringing pride to the Institute.

10.2 BOARD OF STUDIES-OPERATION Board of Studies - Operations is a key non-standing committee of the Institute, focused on providing world-class training to Chartered Accountancy (CA) students. It conducts mandatory training programs such as Orientation, Information Technology (IT), Advanced IT, and Management & Communication Skills through IT and Soft Skills Centres across India. The Board also organizes Four-Week Residential Programs at Centres of Excellence in Hyderabad and Jaipur, along with various student-focused events including International, National, State, and Branch-level Conferences, seminars, workshops, sports competitions, festivals, industrial visits, counselling sessions, and national talent contests. Scholarships are provided to eligible CA students under various categories. The Board aims to equip students with essential professional and interpersonal skills—such as management, communication, presentation, and oratory skills—to meet evolving industry demands and enhance the overall image of the CA profession.

Initatives:

Information Technology Training are being conducted by the IT labs across the country.

10.3 COMMITTEE ON CAREER COUNSELLING The Committee on Career Counselling of ICAI is constituted to promote the Commerce Education with special focus on CA Course amongst the students of class VIII, Secondary, Higher/Senior Secondary, Graduate- Post Graduate. The Prime objective of the Committee is to provide career guidance to the students studying from class VIII till Graduate/Post-Graduate.

Significant Achievements

(I) MOU Signed between ICAI and CBSEFollowing the MoU signed on 21st November 2024 between ICAI and the Department of Skill Education, CBSE, to promote commerce-based skill courses among CBSE students, an Exposure Visit was conducted on 23rd–24th May 2025 at ICAI's Centre of Excellence (COE) and Jaipur Branch. This two-day program offered 27 CBSE school principals from 11 states an in-depth overview of the New Scheme of Education and Training for the Chartered Accountancy (CA) Course. The sessions equipped principals with the knowledge and tools to effectively guide and inspire students to pursue CA as a prestigious and promising career. Participants also experienced ICAI's practical training facilities and gained insight into its expansive network of 177 branches nationwide. Highlighting the visit's impact, one principal was so impressed by the course's value and recognition that he immediately enrolled in the CA Intermediate Course.

(II) Career / Mega Career Counselling Programmes The Committee has organized 2,854 Career Counselling Programmes PAN India and counselled 4,98,382 students with the support of Regional Councils and Branches of ICAI on regular basis. The objective of this initiative is to provide career guidance with special focus on CA Course amongst the students from class VIII till Graduate/PostGraduate. This initiative aims to ensure that students from various educational backgrounds are well-informed about the Chartered Accountancy Course and its career prospects, starting from an early stage in their education.(III) Career Counsellors Empowerment Meet The Committee on Career Counselling envisaged to organize Train the Trainers Programmes titled Career Counsellors Empowerment Meet for empaneled Career Counsellors /are willing to become Career Counsellors of ICAI. The objective of the Faculty Development Programme is to standardize uniform delivery of the counselling sessions all across POUs. Total 12 such Meets were conducted across India and 829 counsellors were motivated.

(IV) Organizing Principal's Conclaves The objective to organize the Principal Conclave is to make School / College principals aware about our New Scheme of education & training and to promote commerce education with special focus on CA Course. The Committee successfully organized 3 Principals Conclave and Approx. 442 School/College Principals, Teachers and Academicians were attended the same.

(V) Participation in Career Fairs/ Exhibitions During this period, ICAI participated in 97 Education and Career Fairs/Exhibitions held across various cities nationwide, reaching and benefiting a total of 53,367 students. These events served as valuable platforms to provide career guidance and disseminate comprehensive information about the Chartered Accountancy course to educators, teachers, students, and other key stakeholders.

(VI) Organizing CAFY & Super Mega Career Counselling Programme - The Career in Accounting & Finance for Youth (CAFY) & Super Mega Career Counselling Programme - was organised PAN India on 29th August, 2024. The objective of this event was to organize maximum programmes at a time PAN India to spread the knowledge about accounting as a profession along with propagation of CA Course and ICAI through the support of Regional Councils and Branches. This mega event was organized with significant involvement of 85 POUs i.e. Regional Councils and Branches of the ICAI and total 1, 16,760 students of class VIII to Graduation benefited in 437 programmes across India along with the Principals, Teachers, and Career Counsellors.

(VIII) Organizing ICAI Commerce Olympiad The Committee successfully organized an online ICAI Commerce Olympiad aimed at students from Class VIII to Graduation. This initiative is designed to increase student engagement with ICAI and raise awareness about the Chartered Accountancy (CA) course. As a unique effort by the Institute, the Olympiad provides students with an opportunity to experience a competitive environment while enhancing their knowledge and skills. The Olympiad was conducted on 19th January 2025, with 8,515 students participating out of the 38,569 registered candidates across the Country. The top three Rankers were awarded Rs. 10,000 along with Merit Certificates in recognition of their achievements. Furthermore, the first three rank holders in the ICAI Commerce Olympiad is granted reimbursement in their registration fees for the Foundation / Direct Entry CA Course.

11. Section – 8 Companies

11.1 XBRL For the effective participation of the regulators, technologists, corporates and academicians in the endeavor of the Institute for the promotion of XBRL in India, the Institute has facilitated the incorporation of XBRL India as a section 25 company (currently under Section 8 of Companies Act, 2013) with the main objective of promoting and encouraging the adoption of XBRL in India as the standard for electronic business reporting in India through development of taxonomies, facilitation of education and training on XBRL. Also, XBRL India has taken membership of XBRL International to facilitate and get updates of XBRL filing globally. XBRL India is an established Indian jurisdiction of XBRL International.

Currently, XBRL India is developing and maintaining XBRL taxonomies for Ministry of Corporate Affairs

(MCA).

XBRL filing requirements by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) • Currently, there are two Taxonomies i.e Ind AS Taxonomy and Commercial and Industrial (C&I) Taxonomy which are applicable to the companies:

XBRL India always focused on its key area of operations i.e. development/ maintenance of Taxonomies for filing of financial statements under Companies Act. Accordingly, XBRL India has submitted samples of Instance Documents for testing purpose of taxonomies submitted to MCA in 2022. It was indicated that the beta version of updated taxonomy shortly so that there would be enough time available to the different XBRL vendors to develop their tool and to test the same for filling of financial statements by stakeholders. In addition to development of taxonomies, queries / issues faced by the filers with regard to taxonomies, while converting the Financial Statements to XBRL formats have been resolved.

Development of new Taxonomies Apart from Ind AS & C&I taxonomies applicable to specified class of companies, XBRL India has proposed MCA for development of taxonomies for various sectors. The matter is under review of MCA: • Non-Banking Finance Companies (NBFCs) • Insurance Entities (both for life and non-life insurance companies separately) • Municipal Corporation Sectors Participation in International activities As a part of becoming a member of XBRL International, the contribution of XBRL India has not been limited to India. The Board members of XBRL India regularly represented India at various meetings/events of XBRL International. During the year, Board members participated in the meeting of XBRL International Member Assembly; 13th XBRL Asia Round Table at Japan; and Data Amplified 2024 and XBRL International Conference at Spain. At the Data Amplified 2024 and XBRL International Conference, the Board member presented on the BRSR initiatives in India and the same was appreciated at the conference.

Board of Directors of XBRL International: Chairman, XBRL India and the President, ICAI has been appointed on the Board of Directors of XBRL International. He attended the meeting of Board of Directors of XBRL International held on 28-29 April, 2025.

11.2 ICAI- Accounting Research Foundation (ARF) ICAI Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF) was established in January, 1999 to be developed as an academy for imparting, spreading and promoting knowledge, learning, education and understanding in the various fields related to the profession of accountancy. The following is the detail of projects undertaken by ICAI ARF: • Indian Railways:

Indian Railways, the fourth largest railway network in the world after the United States, Russia and China, is undertaking several initiatives towards Accounting Reforms in line with generally accepted accounting principles.

In its journey of accounting reforms, the Indian Railways had initiated exercise of preparation of its Accrual Based Financial Statements in 2014, marking a major shift towards enhanced financial transparency and accountability.

Since the inception of this initiative, the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) and its Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF) have played a pivotal role in supporting these reforms. ICAI ARF has been instrumental in guiding Indian Railways through the migration to an accrual-based accounting system. ICAI ARF have successfully prepared the Accrual Based Financial Statements for all Zonal Railways and Production Units from the financial year 2015-16 to 2022-23.

Work Completed in last one year • Accrual Based Financial Statements (ABFS) of Indian Railways for the year 2021-22 and 2022-23 submitted. Several initiatives towards environment Sustainability undertaken by the Indian Railways have been reflected in the financial statements for 2022-23. It will be a milestone in the history of Government of India that accrual based to financial statements has been prepared. • Pilot Study to develop framework for Clearance of CWIP and transfer to FAR to be undertaken over NR based on the CWIP data.

Work in progress:

ABFS of Indian Railways for the year 2023-24. • Treasuries and Accounts Department, Government of Tamil Nadu: ICAI ARF has undertaken the task of rewriting of regulatory framework governing Treasury and Accounts in the State, primarily (i) Treasury Code,

(ii) Accounts Code (iii) Pay and Accounts Office Manual and review of all forms used by the State and recommendations on redundancies, improvements, simplification and new forms to be documented as part of the official code documents of the State. The ICAI has completed the project during the year and submitted the same to Commissioner of Treasuries and Accounts on June 19, 2024. • Indian Ports Association (IPA): ICAI ARF has been assigned the task to prepare common accounting framework for 11 major ports for compliance with Major Ports Authorities Act, 2021 and MPA (Accounts & Audit) Rules, 2021. The Common Framework for the preparation of annual accounts for Major Ports and Significant Accounting Policies document for all ports submitted to IPA. Both the documents have been forwarded by the IPA to Ministry of Shipping Port and Waterways (MoPSW) for notifying in consultation with CAG for implementation in all Major Ports and also to notify the timelines for the placement of audited annual accounts of Major Ports. The matter is under consideration of Ministry of Shipping Port and Waterways. • Certificate Course for Accountants of Panchayats & Municipal Bodies: ICAI ARF executed the project of Certificate Courses for Accountants of Panchayats and Municipal Bodies through Board for Local Bodies Accountants Certification (i.e., BLoAC established under ICAI ARF). The Committee on Public and Government Financial Management (CPGFM) of ICAI is actively involved in development of course material and coordination for the conduct of the courses. The courses were formally launched on 16th November 2023 (Audit Diwas) and the registrations of these courses were initiated on 1st December 2023 through a dedicated website https://lba.icaiarf.org.in/ Approx. 3699 candidates have registered for this course during the year. As per structure of course, two offline exams (screening and main exam) are being held. Under screening exam, basic of accounting is covered and under main exam, panchayat/ municipal bodies specific accounting is covered. ICAI exam system has been replicated for the certificate courses exam after little modification (considering less registrations received) but robustness of ICAI exam system is maintained. • Pursuant to the association between ICAI and the Office of the Comptroller & Auditor General (O/o C&AG) of India for the Certificate Courses for Accountants of Panchayats and Municipal Bodies, the ICAI ARF and ICAI undertook imparting training to officials of C&AG involved in audit of Public Sector Undertakings (PSUs) to enhance the skills of O/o C&AG officials and support their professional development. In this regard, first batch of Residential Capacity Building Programme was conducted starting from 5th May 2025 for the officers of C&AG of India. • Re-writing of Tamil Nadu Financial Code, Volume I & II – The Treasuries and Accounts Department, Government of Tamil Nadu awarded the New Project of Re-writing of Tamil Nadu Financial Code, Volume I & II to the ICAI-ARF in October 2024. The Inception Report along with the revised TNFC (draft) has been submitted in March 2025 to Additional Director - Treasuries and Accounts. • New Logo of ICAI ARF - As a Company, ICAI ARF has adopted a Logo at its first meeting held in January 1999 for use on mastheads of stationery and documents to be used by the company. The Logo of ICAI ARF represents as a symbol to promote public identification and recognition as a research institution. Over the years, the ICAI has undertaken and successfully completed several projects. The ICAI ARF has amended its Logo to enable brand building at national and international level.

11.3 ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation (ICAI RVO) is a section 8 Private Company formed by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India to enroll and regulate Registered Valuers or valuer member as its members in accordance with the Companies (Registered Valuers and Valuation) Rules, 2017, and functions incidental thereto.

(I) Educational Course (50 hours) by ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation which is a precondition to become Registered Valuer: • ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation has been focusing on building its membership base and conducting 50-hour Educational Course for its valuer members, which is a precondition for becoming Registered Valuer and preparing educational material for the Educational Course. • In this direction, from 1st July 2024 to 31st May 2025, ICAI RVO has conducted 10 online batches of the 50hour Educational Course for the asset class 'Securities or Financial Assets', 02 online batches for the asset class 'Land and Building' and 01 online batch for the asset class 'Plant and Machinery', attended by members across the country. • Till end of May 2025, a total of 91 batches of Educational Course have been conducted (86 batches for the asset class Securities or Financial Assets, 4 batches for the asset class Land and Building and 1 batch for the asset class Plant and Machinery). • As on 31st May 2025, 5031 members have been trained by ICAI RVO for its 50-hour Educational Course (5005 members under the asset class Securities or Financial Assets, 24 members under the asset class Land and Building and 02 members under the asset class Plant and Machinery).

(II) Online Classes of Educational Course (50 hours) by ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation which is a precondition to become Registered Valuer: • Due to the outbreak of COVID-19, the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India vide its issuance of guidelines dated 29th March 2022, has permitted to conduct the classes of Educational Course through Online mode. IBBI has further extended the validity of these guidelines, till further orders, vide Amendment dated 30th September 2022. • For the time period 1st July 2024 till 31st May 2025, ICAI RVO has conducted online batches of the 50-hour Educational Course as per the following details:

Asset Class No. of Batches Conducted Total Participants Trained Securities or Financial Assets Land and Building Plant and Machinery

(III) Registration of Registered Valuers (RVs) with Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (IBBI):

As on 31st May 2025, the number of RVs registered with the Authority i.e. the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (IBBI) and ICAI RVO are as follows:

No. of RVs registered with ICAI Asset Class No. of RVs registered with IBBI RVO Securities or Financial Assets Plant and Machinery Land and Building

(IV) Online Learning Management System of ICAI RVO ICAI RVO has its Learning Management System (LMS), which is an online portal that has been developed for the purpose of providing a holistic and easily accessible learning platform, catering to the diverse needs of its members. On one hand detailed learning material (including videos) is available on various topics as prescribed by IBBI for the Valuation Examination, duly supplemented by mock tests in Multiple Choice Questions format for practice, and on the other hand, it has content which fosters continuing education for its RV members on latest valuation related topics.

The LMS is updated on a regular basis with the latest presentations and video recordings of the Training Programmes, and, with our recent foray into the other 2 asset classes i.e. Land and Building & Plant and Machinery, educational material for these asset classes is also being added on LMS for the benefit of its members.

(V) Publications ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation has issued the following Publications from July 2024-May 2025: • Quarterly Journal of ICAI RVO (Volume 1, Issue 2), July 2024 • Quarterly Journal of ICAI RVO (Volume 1, Issue 3), October 2024 • Quarterly Journal of ICAI RVO (Volume 1, Issue 4), January 2025 • Quarterly Journal of ICAI RVO (Volume 1, Issue 5), April 2025

(VI) Continuous Educational Program As a part of its Continuous Educational Programme, ICAI RVO has organised Training Programmes on various topics related to valuation for its Registered Valuer members for the Period July 2024-May 2025, as per the following details:

Asset Class No. of Trainings Total Participants Conducted Trained Hours of Trainings Imparted Securities or Financial Assets (SFA) Land and Building (L&B) L&B and SFA L&B, P&M and SFA

(VII) Master Classes on Valuation ICAI RVO also organizes Master Classes on Valuation for its Registered Valuer Members. Expert Faculty take these sessions focusing on various aspects of Valuation such as approaches, application, case studies, challenges and way forward. During this period, 3 Master Classes (13 hours of training imparted to 230 members) were conducted on the on Decoding recent M&A deals, Intangible Asset Valuation, Overview of Digital Assets and their Valuation and Revisiting Fixed Income Securities with Excel.

(VIII) Training Program on Soft Skills for granting COP to Registered Valuers.

ICAI RVO organised 05 online Certificate of Practice Training Programmes on Soft Skills for granting Certificate of Practice (COP) to Registered Valuers for the Period July 2024-May 2025.

(IX) Refresher Training Programmes In addition to the mandatory 50-hour educational course prescribed by the Rules, ICAI RVO also conducts Refresher Training Programs tailored for Primary Members who have yet to successfully clear the Valuation examination—a prerequisite for attaining Registered Valuer status. These Refresher Programs aim to supplement the knowledge acquired through the educational course, catering to Primary Members seeking to enhance their proficiency or remain updated with the latest Valuation syllabus. The ultimate objective of these Refresher Training initiatives is to equip Primary Members with the necessary skills and expertise to successfully navigate and pass the IBBI Valuation Examination. ICAI RVO organised Refresher Training Programmes on 8 Valuation related topics from July 2024-May 2025.

(X) Residential Programme Insolvency & Valuation Standards Board (I & VSB), ICAI in association with ICAI RVO & Indian Institute of Insolvency Professionals of ICAI (IIIPI) and hosted by EIRC, ICAI, organised Residential Programme, IBC 3.0 at BBSR, Odisha (Mayfair Lagoon, Bhubaneswar, Odisha) from 3rd-4th August 2024.

(XI) Webcasts/Webinars conducted by ICAI RVO ICAI RVO has conducted live webcast on Learnings from the findings of Peer Review of Valuation Reports.

(XII) Conferences organised by ICAI RVO Valuation Day Conference on 18th October 2024 ICAI RVO and IOV RVF, in association with IBBI, organised the 'Valuation Day' program on 18th October 2024, in New Delhi. The event witnessed participation from over 100 attendees and was honoured by the presence of Shri Satish Sethi, Executive Director of IBBI, and Shri B.

Sankaranarayanan, General Manager of IBBI. The program included meaningful discussions revolving around valuation under various Sections of the Companies Act, 2013 and IBC, 2016.

(XIII) RESOLVE 2024 ICAI in association with ICAI RVO and IIIPI organised the 2nd International Convention on Insolvency and Valuation themed "Enabling Resolution, Maximizing Value" on 12th - 13th July 2024 at New Delhi which was attended by more than 100 participants. The conference was graced by Hon'ble Justice Ashok Bhushan, Chairperson, NCLT as the Chief Guest and Shri Ravi Mittal, Chairperson, Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India as the Guest of Honour. The event received overwhelming participation from insolvency professionals, registered valuers, regulators, and policymakers including international speakers.

(XIV)Monitoring of Members through Half-Yearly Monitoring Reports, Peer-Review and Inspection Half-Yearly Monitoring Reports • The ICAI RVO Monitoring Policy provides that a member shall submit information about ongoing and concluded engagements as a registered valuer, in the manner and format specified by the Organisation, at least twice a year i.e. as on 31st March and 30th September, stating inter alia, the date of assignment, date of completion and reference number of valuation assignment and valuation report. Accordingly, the members are required to submit the Half Yearly Monitoring Reports within 15 days from the date of closing of the Half-Year. These reports are then compiled by ICAI RVO for onward submission to IBBI. • During the period, the Registered Valuer Members of ICAI RVO submitted their Half-Yearly Monitoring Reports of the Valuation Assignments undertaken by them during the period 1st October 2024 to 31st March

2025.

Peer-Review • Under the directives of IBBI, ICAI RVO has been conducting a Peer Review Exercise of its members. • During the period, ICAI RVO conducted a Peer Review of the 168 valuation reports submitted by registered valuers from October 1, 2022, to September 30, 2023, which also included a Peer Review of Registered Valuer Entities registered with ICAI RVO for the first time. • The Peer Reviewer panel of ICAI RVO meticulously examined 168 valuation reports, carefully assessing them against six crucial criteria, each of which was further dissected into multiple observation points. The successful execution of the peer review exercise by the ICAI RVO, guided by the IBBI, signifies a significant milestone in the relentless pursuit of professional excellence and the overall growth of the valuation profession. • While certain areas for improvement and enhanced compliance still exist, it is noteworthy that a substantial majority of the reviewed reports demonstrated compliance with statutory requirements, and the observations from previous peer reviews were duly incorporated. • The findings of the Peer-Review Exercise which concluded in August 2024 have been shared with the IBBI and are also made available on the website of ICAI RVO at https://icairvo.in/documents-on-peerreview.aspx Inspection of RVs • In compliance with IBBI directives, all Registered Valuers Organisations (RVOs) must conduct inspections of their Registered Valuers (RVs) enrolled up to March 31, 2024, as per their respective Inspection Policies. • Following this requirement, ICAI RVO has issued Notices of Inspection to 61 RVs, requesting the submission of valuation reports and other documents for the Inspection Exercise. The inspection exercise has been concluded, and the final findings of the Inspection are to be placed before the Monitoring Committee of ICAI RVO in its upcoming meeting for the consideration and approval.

11.4 Indian Institute of Insolvency Professionals of ICAI (IIIPI) Indian Institute of Insolvency Professionals of ICAI (IIIPI) has completed a successful year of 2024-25. It was awarded with the registration certificate as the First Insolvency Professional Agency of India by Hon'ble Union Finance Minister Shri Arun Jaitley on 28th November 2016 at Delhi. IIIPI has attracted members from a diverse stream including Chartered Accountants, Company Secretaries, Cost Accountants, Advocates and Management Professionals. IIIPI has also networked with other national and international bodies in furtherance of capacity/knowledge building of stakeholders on one hand and providing value-added policy inputs for evolving law and practice of IBC, on the other. Out of total 4507 Insolvency Professionals registered with IBBI as on 30th April 2025, 2834, i.e., 62.88% are from IIIPI. IIIPI's members have so far handled ~75% of the overall assignments of CIRP/Liquidation/Voluntary Liquidation, including many of the largest cases.

(I) Capacity Building Initiatives:

The dialogues and deliberations are crucial to the capacity building of professional members in any profession. It is even more pertinent in the context of the insolvency profession, which impacts multiple stakeholders and is considered one of the most complex professions. On the shoulders of an insolvency professional as an important pillar, lie critical responsibilities, which are onerous at times, during and around the process of rescuing the corporate lives. In this backdrop it can be said that the performance of the IBC ecosystem, to a great extent, is directly linked to the capacity building of IPs. As the key focus, IIIPI's endeavour has been to not only sustain the momentum but also keep launching several capacity building initiatives to meet the expectations of our stakeholders. Following are some of the key initiatives: • 18 Nos. of Executive Development Programs (EDPs-virtual) in 2 to 5 days format, across five domains viz.

Managing CD as Going Concern, Legal Skills, Forensic Skills for Avoidance Transactions, Cross Border Insolvency and Group Insolvency. The last 2 categories have been added since August 2024 onwards. • 43 Nos. of Webinars covering multiple contemporary topics conducted, as 3-hours format. • 10 Nos. of one-day weekend workshops covering multiple topics. • 7 Nos. of Roundtables (virtual) on discussion papers by IBBI and emerging issues. • 8 Nos. of LIE Exam preparation classes (virtual) across 5-days format. • 15 Half-day workshops (virtual) in association with IBBI and other IPAs on Common issues • 4 physical half-day workshops in association with IBBI and other IPAs on Common issues • The International Conference (physical) on Cross Border & Group Insolvency was conducted in association with UK-FCDO on April 13th, 2024. • International Convention 'Resolve' (physical) was conducted by IVSB-ICAI in association with IIIPI at Bharat Mandapam on 12th/13th July 2024. • Joined EIRC-ICAI for organizing 2 day-Conclave on IBC at Bhubaneswar in Aug.' 2024 • Physical Conference to present reports on (i) Proposals for Amendments in IBC, & (ii) Regulatory Framework for IPs/IPEs, in Delhi during Sept. 2024. • IIIPI Foundation Day Conference (physical) on Nov. 26, 2024. • Joined Insol International UK, in organizing the physical-International Conference on IBC Mumbai on Feb. 8,

2025. • Joined NALSAR Hyderabad for organizing 2-days physical research conference in Hyderabad during April

2025. • One day workshop (virtual) on Industry sector training (Real Estate and EPC industries) in association of CARE Ratings, conducted on July 30, 2024. • Peer Review exercise of IIIPI members is picking up in nos. So far 34 successful peer reviews have been completed, 49 more are in pipeline. Further, 43 peer-reviewers have been registered. • Making available membership of INSOL International UK to IIIPI's members at a subsidized cost. 162 members have opted for this. • Regular release of quarterly journal "The Resolution Professional" is a credible mouthpiece and brand ambassador for IIIPI. • Weekly Newsletters and Case-Law Capsule dissemination electronically. • IIIPI set up a stall (through bronze sponsorship) at World Forum of Accountants (WOFA) Conference organized by ICAI on Jan. 31-Feb.2, 2025 in New Delhi. • National level Quiz on IBC conducted with result announced during April 2024 at a conference.

(II) Research and Studies Conducted • Since April'24, eleven studies/reports through Study groups have been finalized and disseminated in respect of: ➢ Template for forming/submitting opinion on Avoidance Transactions ➢ Best Practices for Conducting COC/SCC Meetings by IPs ➢ Developing Market for Stressed Assets in India ➢ Improving real-estate resolutions and coordination with RERA. Basis this interactions have been held with All India Forum of RERA Chairmen and MOHUA. Further, a physical conference on the subject was also conducted on 17th September,2024 ➢ Survey report on IPs' interface with statutory authorities and enforcement agencies. ➢ Timely Turnaround: Bottleneck in CIRP and Liquidations – Case Study ➢ Removing duplicity/redundancies in compliances by IPs. Basis this IBBI has released discussion paper on the matter. ➢ Key Recommendations on Amendments in IBC ➢ Recommendation on Regulatory Framework for IPs/IPEs ➢ Legal Digest of Disciplinary Cases ➢ Common Issues Observed During Inspection • 5 research projects have been approved so far by IIIPI under its research project scheme. These are in the areas of Real Estate Resolutions, PPIRP framework, Insolvency Framework in India Vs. Internationally, and resolving insolvency in FPCs in East India.

(III) Monitoring of Insolvency Professionals:

As per Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (Model Byelaws and Governing Board of Insolvency Professional Agencies) Regulations, 2016, IIIPI is required to monitor the professional activities and conduct of professional members for their adherence to the provisions of the Code, Rules, Regulations and Guidelines issued there-under, the byelaws, the Code of Conduct and directions given by the Governing Board. Insolvency Professionals are monitored on an ongoing basis with respect to Corporate Insolvency Resolution Process and Liquidation Process. Actions are being taken against the members who have not complied with the applicable provisions.

(IV) GRC /Disciplinary Mechanism:

IIIPI has received 11 Grievances during the period from 01st April 2024 to 31st March 2025 and a total of 26 Grievances (including Grievances from previous year) were redressed during the period by the Grievance Redressal Committee and as on end of 31st May 2025 there are pendency of 3 GRC matters. Further, 100 (hundred) disciplinary proceedings were initiated by the IIIPI during the period from 01st April 2024 to 31st March 2025 and as on 31st May 2025, 95 (ninety-five) matters were disposed of.

11.5 The Institute of Social Auditors of India (ISAI) The Institute of Social Auditors of India (ISAI) is incorporated under Section 8 of the Companies Act, 2013 on October 07, 2022 under the aegis of The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) as Self-Regulatory Organization with prime objective to be a leading institution for developing an independent, ethical and world-class Social Impact Assessors (earlier Social Auditors) responding to needs and expectations of the stakeholders. ISAI enrol, regulate, and develop Social Impact Assessors (earlier Social Auditors) in an independent and transparent manner and also work on capacity building of the Social Impact Assessors through continuous professional education and training.

(I) Concept of Social Impact Assessors (earlier Social Auditors) The Finance Minister in the Budget speech of 2019-2020 for the first time proposed the concept of a Social Stock Exchange which will be "under the regulatory ambit of Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) for listing social enterprises and voluntary organizations working for the realization of a social welfare objective so that they can raise capital as equity, debt or as units like a mutual fund." Subsequently, the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) vide its notification dated 25th July 2022 has amended the SEBI (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2018 ("ICDR Regulations"), SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 ("LODR Regulations") and SEBI (Alternative Investment Funds) Regulations, 2012 ("AIF Regulations") to provide a broad framework for Social Stock Exchange. SEBI (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) (Third Amendment) Regulations, 2022 defines Social Auditors as an individual registered with a Self Regulatory Organization under the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India or such other agency, as may be specified by the Board, who has qualified a certification program conducted by National Institute of Securities Market and holds a valid certificate. SEBI (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirement) (Third Amendment) Regulation, 2023 dated December 21, 2023 substituted the word "Auditor" appearing in the words and expression

"Social Auditor" with the words "Impact Assessor".

(II) Governing Board of ISAI ISAI functions through its Governing Board which comprises of minimum of Twelve Directors i.e. • Chairperson, having rich experience in social sector/ impact assessment or he may be from a renowned academic institution. • Five Central Council members of ICAI/ Sustainability Reporting Standards Board with relevant experience. • One person to be nominated by Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI). • One person to be nominated by Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA)/ Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)/ Ministry of Social Justice & Empowerment. • One person nominated by Capacity Building Fund. • One person nominated by Social Stock Exchange. • One member from thematic areas of the social sector. • One member having cross sectoral experience in social sectors.

(III) Governing Board meetings:

For the period April 01, 2024 to March 31, 2025, 3 (Three) Governing Board meetings were convened at the Registered office of the Company. The 2nd Annual General Meeting(AGM) of the Company was held on September 30, 2024. The Annual Return and financials have been filed with MCA.

(IV) Sub-Committees of ISAI There are 7 Sub-Committees constituted in terms of the bye-laws of the Company to regulate the working of the Company in the most transparent and efficient manner. These sub committee comprises of members of the Governing Board and also market experts in the advisory role. Following are the sub committees of ISAI i.e Advisory Committee, Membership Committee, Monitoring Committee, Grievance Redressal Committee, Appellate Committee, Disciplinary Committee and Editorial Board. During the Financial year 2024-2025, four Membership Committee meetings were convened on May 08, 2024, July 16, 2024, October 16, 2024 and January 20, 2025 through virtual/physical mode wherein important decisions related to allotment of membership of ISAI were taken.

(V) Registration of Social Impact Assessors (earlier Social Auditors) and Social Impact Assessment Entities ISAI has registered 700 Social Impact Assessors (earlier Social Auditor), the details of which is available on the website of the Company. The registration link for Social Impact Assessment Entities was made live on the website of ISAI on September 03, 2024.

(VI) Online Training Course on "NISM Series XXIII: Social Impact Assessor (earlier Social Auditors) Certification Examination" ISAI conducts online training course to facilitate preparation for "NISM Series XXIII: Social Impact Assessor (earlier Social Auditors) Certification Examination". The online classes cover all 11 chapters of the NISM Study material covered in 21 study hours with expert faculties, MCQs for each chapters, case studies and illustrative examples. ISAI has completed two (2) batches of online training course during the financial year 2024-2025 and trained 89 participants for the "NISM Series XXIII: Social Impact Assessor (earlier Social Auditors) Certification Examination".

(VII) Webinars ISAI has organized seven webinars for capacity building and creating awareness about the Social Stock Exchange, Social Audit and Social impact Assessment with the stakeholders and to the public at large during the year.

12. Quality Review Board (QRB) The Quality Review Board (QRB) has been constituted by the Central Government of India u/s 28A of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 as an independent oversight body engaged in monitoring the quality of services provided by the Chartered Accountants in India. The constitution of the QRB includes a chairperson, 5 members appointed by the Central Government and 5 members nominated from the Council of ICAI. Although, the provisions of Sec. 28B of the Act authorizes the QRB to review all kinds of services provided by the members of the ICAI, however, the Board currently is involved in review of audit services, only. As per Section 28B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, the Quality Review Board shall perform the following functions: - • To make recommendations to the Council with regard to the quality of services provided by the members of the Institute. • To review the quality of services provided by the members of the Institute including audit services. • To guide the members of the Institute to improve the quality of services and adherence to the various statutory and other regulatory requirements. • To forward cases of non-compliance with various statutory and regulatory requirements by the members of the Institute or firms, noticed by it during the course of its reviews, to the Disciplinary Directorate for its examination.

After the formation of National Financial Reporting Authority (NFRA) by the Central Government in the year 2018, the QRB has been involved in review of the quality of the audit services w.r.t entities not falling under Rule 3 of NFRA Rules, 2018. In order to make the process of quality reviews more comprehensive the Board has taken following key steps during the year 2024-25: • Modification in the criteria of selection of companies / audit firms in order to make the selection process more robust and inclusive. Modified selection criteria can be referred at "Procedure for Quality Review of Audit Services of Audit Firms (Revised)" hosted at website of the Board www.qrbca.in • Modification in the "Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) for conducting Quality Reviews", thus reducing the overall time taken in completion of audit quality reviews. The revised SOP is hosted at the website of the Board.

During the period covered by the Report, 52 reviews of audit quality have been conducted by the Quality Review Board and Quality Review Group I & II and out of these 52 reviews:

S. No.

Particulars Review of audit quality

1.

Number of cases for which advisories to concerned Audit firms for improvement of the quality of audit services and adherence to the various statutory and other regulatory requirements in terms of the requirements of clause (c) of Section 28B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, were recommended/ decided.

2.

Number of cases recommended/ decided to be forwarded to the Disciplinary Directorate of ICAI for its examination in terms of the requirements of clause (d) of Section 28B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949.

3.

Number of cases filed i.e., cases where no action was taken Total cases During the year covered by the Report following meetings were held: • Four Meetings of Quality Review Board, • Three Meeting of Quality Review Group – I constituted by QRB, • Four Meeting of Quality Review Group – II constituted by QRB, • One Meeting of Sub-Committee – I constituted by QRB, • One Meeting of Audit Committee of QRB.

The Council wishes to place on record its appreciation of the continued assistance and support given by the Central Government and its nominees on the Council during the year 2024-25.

The Council also acknowledges its appreciation of the sincere and devoted efforts put in during the year 2024-25 and thereafter by all officers and employees of the ICAI.
